# MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R001` | **Outbox head-only attempt accounting and rewind.** The outbox increments retry attempts only for the row currently at the head of a stream, so a failing head cannot exhaust the attempt budget of healthy successor rows. A consumer can declare an explicit reject, which dead-letters the head immediately and reports the transition. An administrator can list head status and dead letters. The administrator can also issue a rewind to replay from an earlier position. Caller chain: the outbox consumer's lease call runs `claim` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs` (head test at `head_only_attempt`, line 698). `MutationKernel::outbox_reject` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/operator.rs` calls `reject` at `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/cursor.rs:336`. The operator reaches head status, dead letters and rewind through `Method::MutationOutbox` → `handle_mutation_outbox` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/mutation_outbox.rs:35`) → `operate_outbox` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/views.rs:111`) and `rewind` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rewind.rs:77`). Shape: no new type. The rule lives in `head_only_attempt`, `try_dead_letter_head` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs:535`), `dead_letters` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/dead_letters.rs:26`) and `rewind`, all over the generic `OwnerDomain`, so every outbox owner gets it. Wire views are `OutboxDeadLetterPage` and `OutboxRewindReceipt` of `medulla-mutation-types`. | Outbox tests prove three behaviours. A failing head never dead-letters a healthy successor, and repeated head releases dead-letter only the head. A consumer reject dead-letters immediately, and rewind replays. Tests: `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::a_failing_head_never_dead_letters_a_healthy_successor` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::reject_dead_letters_the_head_at_once_and_the_stream_continues` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::rewind_redelivers_in_order_from_a_mid_stream_target` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R002` | **Outbox consumers get per-class topics and reject-driven rebuild.** Outbox consumers publish to semantically distinct topics per message class. A reasoning-projection consumer that rejects a message triggers a rebuild rather than silently acknowledging a skipped projection. `MutationOutbox` exposes an administrative method for inspecting and managing consumer state. The outbox reports metrics on attempts, rejects, dead letters, and rebuilds. Caller chain: the reasoning-projection worker `sweep` in `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection/recovery.rs` calls `reject_invalid` (line 45) and `rebuild_after_skipped` (line 25) when `settle`/`resolve` of `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs` sees a rejected claim. The operator view is `MutationKernel::outbox_dead_letters` and `outbox_rewind` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/operator.rs`. Metrics register in `crates/medulla-kernel/src/metrics/outbox.rs`. Shape: topics are the `topic` strings validated by `validate_topic` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs:245`); one consumer name binds one topic (`subscribed_topic`, `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/stream.rs:65`). Counters are `medulla_outbox_dead_lettered_total`, `medulla_outbox_head_age_ms` and `medulla_outbox_head_attempt` in `metrics/outbox.rs`; the rebuild count reuses that file and adds no second registry. | Verified by outbox integration tests asserting per-class topic routing, reject-triggered rebuild of a reasoning projection, and exported outbox metrics. Tests: `crates/medulla-h-retrieve/src/reasoning_projection.rs::a_dead_lettered_claim_rebuilds_the_projection_before_further_reads` (L2); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::two_topics_retry_exhaustion_preserves_other_topic_attempts_and_progress` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R003` | **Owner-store layout lineage registry with typed upgrade refusal.** The owner-store layout maintains a lineage registry that recognizes every supported on-disk format and refuses an unsupported layout with a named `{LAYOUT}_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` error rather than reinterpreting it silently. A generated document records the steps required to remove a retired layout from the registry. Caller chain: every owner open (`bind_serving_scope`, `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs:306`) checks the manifest digest against `layout_predecessors` and `pinned_layout_digest` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` (lines 266 and 326). The refusal string comes from `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/persisted_layout.rs:45`. The operator command `store-upgrade` (`src/operator_command.rs`) runs the listed upgrades. Shape: `OwnerLayout` and `LayoutPredecessor` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` hold the registry (`ALL_LAYOUTS`, 18 layouts). `persisted_layout.rs` derives `{LAYOUT}_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` from the canonical layout name. The removal-steps document is rendered in `lineage.rs` (line 455) through `render_removal_step` of `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/offline_upgrade.rs`; no second table exists. | Verified by a golden lineage test that loads each supported manifest format and checks the typed refusal for unsupported ones. Tests: `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/tests.rs::the_graph_store_open_names_the_command_for_an_upgradable_shard` (L3); `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/tests.rs::every_registered_upgrade_is_one_the_command_can_run` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R004` | **Outbox attempt accounting is scoped to the head row.** Re-leasing an outbox row increments the attempt count only for the row at the head of its stream. Healthy successor rows staged behind it never gain an attempt. A failing head cannot dead-letter its successors. This accounting is implemented in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs`. Caller chain: `claim` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs` re-leases a row and calls `head_only_attempt` (line 698); the dead-letter step is `try_dead_letter_head` (line 535) → `dead_letter` (line 648). The served entry is the reasoning-projection consumer `sweep` in `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs:97`. Shape: the one helper `head_only_attempt(attempt, is_head)` returns the attempt unchanged for a successor and `saturating_add(1)` for the head. The head budget is `MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS` (16) at `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs:66`. No second counter exists for a successor. | One test asserts a failing head never dead-letters a healthy successor. Another test asserts sixteen re-leases of the head dead-letter only the head. Tests: `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::a_failing_head_never_dead_letters_a_healthy_successor` (L1); `crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/outbox.rs::releasing_the_head_sixteen_times_dead_letters_it` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R005` | **Outbox delivery state remains local to the owning node.** The local store owner writes outbox delivery-side rows. The multi-group raft layer does not replicate them. Delivery progress stays node-local and outside the replicated log. Caller chain: the local owner writes delivery rows through `claim` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/claim.rs:57`) and `ack` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/cursor.rs:42`) on its own `OwnedStoreHandle`. The raft apply path `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/store/apply.rs` (lines 90 and 117) names outbox authority as owner-local and has no command that carries a delivery row. Shape: no new type. Delivery rows stay in the owner file tables of `crates/medulla-transaction/src/outbox/rows.rs`; no `NativeMutationCommand` variant in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/` carries one. The test reads the follower state through the existing cluster harness of `crates/medulla-server-cluster-tests`. | Confirmed by an integration test checking that outbox delivery rows are not present in replicated state on non-owning cluster members. Test: `crates/medulla-server-cluster-tests/src/split_tests/raft_cluster.rs::outbox_delivery_rows_are_absent_on_non_owning_members` (L3, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006` | **Engine startup does not block on one store's lazy open.** Engine startup must not refuse entirely when the agent-library store fails to open. That store opens lazily. A failure to open it affects only the operations that depend on it, not overall startup. Caller chain: the server boot reaches the Agent Library only through `ensure_agent_library` (`crates/medulla-persistence-records/src/agent_library.rs:709`) → `open_shared_agent_library` (line 724), called from the admin handlers (`crates/medulla-h-admin-schema/src/admin/agent.rs:51`) and the projection worker. Boot (`crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs`) never calls it before the listeners bind. Shape: no new type. The lazy open is the `agent_library_open` slot of `ServerState`; a failed open returns `Err(String)` to the one request and leaves the slot empty so the next request retries. The test reuses the boot wiring of `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs`. | A startup test forces the agent-library store to fail its open. The test asserts that the engine still starts and serves unrelated operations. Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs::boot_serves_unrelated_operations_when_the_agent_library_open_fails` (L3, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.1` | **A clean reopen writes nothing, and a fresh shard is ready in one commit.** Open proves an already-bound serving scope in a read transaction. It begins no write transaction for that proof. The bind refuses a read-only open before that proof, because a bound scope is write authority. Ledger bootstrap checks the declared ledger tables under a write capability, then aborts. It commits nothing and issues no fsync. A fresh owner file binds its control scope inside the one create commit. Create, bind and ledger bootstrap therefore cost one commit. The engine opens the three sidecar stores beside the graph shards. They are `admin-mutations.redb`, `node_info.redb` and `cluster_hierarchy.redb`. A refused open removes every sidecar file that attempt created. Caller chain: `RedbBackend` open (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/backend_open.rs`: `prepare_shards`, line 51, and `open_sidecars`, line 104) → `bind_serving_scope` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs:306`) → `bind_serving_scope_in` (line 605). Shape: the bind proof is a read transaction in `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs`; the create path reuses the one create commit of the physical layer in `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/`. `open_sidecars` opens `admin-mutations.redb`, `node_info.redb` and `cluster_hierarchy.redb`; a refused open removes the files that attempt created. | Storage-kernel tests reopen a bound store and assert the file bytes stay unchanged after the bind. A create-bound store reports its scope bound without a second commit. A K=8 backend test asserts every sidecar file exists after open. A second test refuses a shard open and asserts no new sidecar file remains. Tests: `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::reopening_a_bound_store_writes_nothing` (L1); `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::sidecars_open_with_the_shards_and_a_refusal_leaves_none` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.2` | **Startup open has a time budget that holds under host load, and each open is diagnosable.** The budget runs in one process on a real disk, beside 16 fsync-writing threads and 32 CPU-burn threads. Under that load a fresh K=8 store opens within 4 s. A small reopened K=8 store opens within 2 s. A populated reopen of about 300 MB on its markers opens within 2.5 s. A separate stress check runs ten copies in parallel, each with its own load. Its looser limits are 60 s fresh and 45 s for each reopen. Every kernel-owned store open logs one line with its duration and phase split. The phases are file open, table validation, recovery scan or skip, bind and ledger. The same split feeds the `medulla_store_open_phase_seconds` histogram. The `medulla_store_open_recovery_scan_total` counter records the scan outcome. Caller chain: `RedbBackend` open (`backend_open.rs`) → store opens in `crates/medulla-storage/src/kernel.rs`; the phase log and histogram come from `crates/medulla-kernel/src/metrics.rs` (`medulla_store_open_phase_seconds`, line 343; `medulla_store_open_recovery_scan_total`, line 350). Shape: the gate is `tests/shard_open_budget.rs` (helpers `check`, `best`, `finish`, scenarios `small_scenario` and `populated_scenario`); no production type is added beyond the phase timers in the kernel open path. | `tests/shard_open_budget.rs` builds the fresh, small and populated K=8 stores under that load. It asserts each budget from the open call's own wall clock, on the fastest of three independent opens: other work on a shared host only adds time to one open, while a real slowdown adds it to all three. The populated reopen asserts from its own phase lines that every owner store of every marker reopen skipped the recovery scan, and that removing the markers brings the scan back on every shard; its 2.5 s wall budget is a performance claim held only on a quiet host under the bench gate (`MEDULLA_SHARD_OPEN_BENCH=1`) and by the stress check, because co-tenant load alone stretches file open past it. A missed limit prints the captured per-store phase lines. The build host runs the gate ten times in a row with no failure. The ignored stress test runs the ten parallel copies. Tests: `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::shard_open_stays_within_budget_under_load` (L3); `tests/shard_open_budget.rs::populated_reopen_hits_the_marker_fast_path` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.3` | **A clean-shutdown marker lets open skip the full recovery scan.** A graceful close writes a marker file beside the owner file. The marker binds the store incarnation and the physical identity. It also binds the file length, the modification time and the redb header page. That header page carries the last commit. Open skips the row scan of `validate_recovery_content` only when the marker matches the file. A skipped open still checks the persisted root, every scope binding and version row, and every fence's stamped identity. The marker vouches only for content that passed the scan. A session may create the file or commit a write. Its close then re-runs the scan read-only. The close writes the marker only when that scan passes. redb rewrites the header when it opens a file for write. A writable open that found a valid marker therefore stamps it again against that header. The first commit after an open durably removes the marker before redb's commit starts. No commit can become durable while a marker exists. A crash after any commit therefore leaves no valid marker. An aborted write commits nothing, so it leaves the marker in place. The ledger bootstrap on every open is such a write. An absent, torn, copied, restored, upgraded or externally rewritten file runs the full scan. The marker speeds up startup and proves no integrity. Rows tampered with behind a valid marker are out of scope. Caller chain: the owner open → `validate_recovery_content` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/validate.rs:79`) asks `is_valid` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:80`); a graceful close writes the marker at the path of `marker_path` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:50`). Shape: `CleanMarker` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/clean_marker.rs:103`) binds incarnation, identity, file length, modification time and the redb header page. The first-commit removal sits in the write authority of `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/write_authority.rs`. | Storage-kernel tests cover each case. A clean reopen reports the scan skipped. A missing marker, a torn marker, a copied marker and an external commit each run the scan. Beginning a write keeps the marker. The commit removes it, and the next close restores it. An aborted write keeps a valid marker. An aborted ledger bootstrap keeps it, and the next open still skips the scan. A child process killed by `abort()` after a write leaves no marker, so the reopen scans. A child killed without writing keeps its still-true marker. A store closed with a foreign fence writes no marker. A forged matching marker still lets the fence check refuse the reopen. Tests: `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::a_store_with_no_marker_runs_the_full_scan` (L1); `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::the_first_commit_after_open_removes_the_marker_before_committing` (L1); `crates/medulla-storage/src/physical/tests.rs::a_process_killed_after_a_write_leaves_no_valid_marker` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.4` | **Pre-listener boot does not block on write-lock-holding maintenance.** The projection drain never holds the global write lock across the agent-library store open. The worker ensures the store outside the lock. Publishing the ensured handle takes one short write. A re-check lets a concurrent ensurer win. Per-row critical sections stay short. No write spans a projection graph commit. Each non-empty sweep logs one INFO line: rows claimed, graphs committed, elapsed. The worker never performs minutes of work silently. The matview reload and the ts reconciliation close RAM re-hydration and crash-window residuals. Nothing requires them to precede serving. They run as background tasks spawned after the primary listeners bind. Each logs its start and completion: counts and elapsed. Code roots: `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`, `crates/medulla-server/src/transport.rs`, `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs`, `src/main.rs`. Caller chain: `serve_transports` in `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs` (lines 808 and 830) calls `spawn_post_bind_maintenance` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs:35`) after the listeners bind; the projection worker `run` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/connector_pack_projection.rs:77`) ensures the store in `open_agent_library` (line 153) outside the write lock. Shape: no new type. `sweep` (line 97) logs one INFO line per non-empty sweep; `spawn_matview_reload` and `spawn_time_series_reconcile` in `boot_maintenance.rs` are the background tasks. | The test seeds N pending import rows on a temp durable store. It boots the real wiring: projection worker, UDS listener, post-bind maintenance. It asserts the listener binds within 30s and before the drain completes. It asserts the drain finishes within a bounded wait. Test: `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/split_tests/medulla_h_admin_boot_regression.rs::boot_listener_binds_before_the_projection_drain_completes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R006.5` | **Ts startup reconciliation replays the legacy gap once, then uses the cursor fast path.** A legacy store lacks durable projection cursors. Every series then takes the slow path once. The slow path is a full range scan plus exact multiset point diff. The pass logs its start: series count per authoritative shard. It logs completion: series examined and reconciled, points replayed, cursors written, elapsed. It logs even when the work is zero. `mark_projection_ready` persists the (count, min_ts, max_ts) cursor durably. Subsequent boots skip a converged series without a point scan. The replay never duplicates a point. The move off the pre-listener path does not change diff correctness. Code roots: `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs`, `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`. Caller chain: `spawn_post_bind_maintenance` → `spawn_time_series_reconcile` (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/boot_maintenance.rs`) → `reconcile_time_series` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs:186`) → `reconcile_shard` (line 140) → `reconcile_series` (line 89). Shape: the cursor is the `(count, min_ts, max_ts)` triple written by `mark_projection_ready` in `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/timeseries.rs`; the diff is the existing exact multiset diff of `reconcile_series`. | It seeds a series durable in the authoritative shard with an empty served store. It asserts the reconciliation replays exactly the seeded points within a bounded wait. It asserts a second reconciliation is a true no-op: zero replay, no duplicates. Test: `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/redb_backend_tests.rs::ts_reconcile_replays_seeded_points_once_then_is_a_no_op` (L2, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007` | **Host binary exposes offline store, archive and restore commands.** The host binary `medulla-server` provides `store-upgrade inspect` and `store-upgrade apply` in `src/operator_command.rs`. They examine and migrate on-disk layout state outside normal server startup. The binary also provides the `archive` tool and the `restore` options `--to-seq` and `--to-time` (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007.1`). Caller chain: `medulla-server store-upgrade inspect|apply` → `Cli::run` in `src/operator_command.rs:65` → the store-upgrade report of `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/`; `restore` and `migrate-shards` dispatch through `run_if_requested` (`src/offline_tools.rs`). Shape: the `TOOLS` table in `src/offline_tools.rs` lists the offline tools; `src/operator_command.rs` owns `store-upgrade`. The `archive` tool and the restore targets are specified by R007.1; nothing else is added here. | Verified by a CLI integration test exercising `store-upgrade inspect` and `store-upgrade apply` against a sample data directory. Tests: `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::storage_inspect_reports_nothing_to_do_on_an_empty_data_directory` (L3); `tests/operator_command_store_upgrade_cli.rs::storage_apply_without_confirm_is_refused_and_opens_nothing` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R007.1` | **Offline tools archive and restore to a target.** Stage: durable kernel, offline tools. Caller chain (restore): `medulla-server restore --bundle B --persist-dir D --shards K --archive <backend-spec> --to-seq N | --to-time RFC3339` → `src/offline_tools/restore.rs::run` → `backup::restore_bundle` → `recovery::archive::replay_to`. Caller chain (archive): `medulla-server archive --persist-dir D --to <backend-spec>` → new `src/offline_tools/archive.rs::run`, registered in `TOOLS` of `src/offline_tools.rs`. Restore keeps its rule that the destination holds no shard files. Without a target it behaves as today. Consumer: `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R016.2`. Caller chain: `medulla-server restore` → `run` (`src/offline_tools/restore.rs:47`) → `restore_bundle` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/backup.rs:801`) → `replay_to` in the new `crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/archive.rs`; `medulla-server archive` → the new `src/offline_tools/archive.rs::run`. Shape: the archive tool is a new file beside `restore.rs` and a new entry in `TOOLS` of `src/offline_tools.rs`. `replay_to(archive, seq_or_time)` lives with the recovery modules of `crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/` and reuses the bundle manifest read of `backup.rs`. `restore_bundle` keeps its fresh-directory rule and gains an optional target argument. | Test: `tests/offline_restore_pitr_cli.rs::target_past_archive_refuses_and_writes_nothing` (L3, planned). Test: `tests/offline_restore_pitr_cli.rs::target_past_archive_refuses_and_writes_nothing` (L3, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R008` | **Raft unit tests pass under cluster features and run in CI.** All `raft::*` unit tests and `txn_reconcile_ack_lost_retry` pass with cluster features, without recursion-depth failures. A CI job compiles and runs the raft suite on every change. Caller chain: the CI job `gates (slim server + raft cluster)` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (line 649) runs the `Test (raft cluster ...)` steps at lines 680 and 682 on every main push. The steps call `scripts/cargo_test_rescue.py cargo test --locked -p medulla-engine --features cluster,harness,calvin --lib -- raft::`; the guard test is `tests/test_raft_cluster_ci_gate.py`. | Verified by a green continuous-integration job that builds with cluster features and executes the raft test suite, including `txn_reconcile_ack_lost_retry`. Tests: `tests/test_raft_cluster_ci_gate.py::test_gates_variants_job_builds_and_tests_raft_under_cluster_features` (L2); `tests/test_raft_cluster_ci_gate.py::test_gates_variants_job_runs_txn_reconcile_ack_lost_retry` (L2). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R009` | **Native raft commands are sealed as their bare method.** Native consensus commands are sealed and opened as the bare method they represent. That happens in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/sealed.rs` (`SealedNativeMethod::seal_value`/`open_value`). A native raft command payload is therefore never rejected over a mismatched envelope. Caller chain: a native raft command is proposed through `NativeMutationCommand` (`crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/mod.rs`) → `SealedNativeMethod::seal_value` (`crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/sealed.rs:23`); the apply side opens it through `open_value` (line 46). Shape: `SealedNativeMethod` in `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/sealed.rs` is the one sealing type; the bare method is the sealed payload, so no envelope field can mismatch. | Verified by a follower catch-up test that replays a create-graph-then-write sequence and by unit tests on `SealedNativeMethod` sealing and opening. Tests: `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/tests.rs::native_envelopes_reject_empty_unsealed_malformed_oversize_and_wrong_key` (L1); `crates/medulla-raft/src/raft/command/native/tests.rs::work_item_mutations_round_trip_through_sealed_native_command` (L1). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R010` | **jscpd clone gate runs on every commit.** The jscpd structural-clone gate runs in the per-commit check suite. The gate flags each new duplicate code pair that an extraction introduces. Caller chain: the `jscpd-differential` hook of `.config/pre-commit.yaml` runs `scripts/check_duplication.py enforce --base-ref` against the base from `scripts/gate_base_ref.sh`. Shape: no new file. The hook config of `.config/pre-commit.yaml` names the gate; `scripts/check_duplication.py` reads the committed tree and reports each new clone pair. | The per-commit hook configuration invokes the jscpd gate. The gate catches a deliberately duplicated extraction. Test: `tests/test_check_duplication.py::test_enforce_flags_a_new_duplicate_pair` (L2, planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R011` | **Required runtime and clippy gates run on every pull request (rollup).** Subsystem: release gates. Caller chain: the `ci.yml` pull-request job calls the shared gate of PIPE-GATES-R017. The wire protocol, capability policy and isolation layers serve propose/approve/revoke/get/list through the request boundary, with the acting identity server-stamped from the verified request context rather than trusted from the request body. Reads are tenant-scoped: `GovernedLedger::get(change_id, tenant, now_ms)` and `list(tenant, now_ms)` return only changes whose `proposer_tenant` equals the caller's verified tenant, and a foreign `Get` answers byte-identically to an unknown id. Every governed decision takes its time from the `medulla_types::clock::Clock` the `IsolationLayer` holds (`governed_now_ms`): the service installs `EngineClock` (the authoritative time of the command) through `with_governed_clock`, and a test installs a `ManualClock` and advances it to close a window with no wall-clock wait. The layer carries no test seam. Caller chain: the request boundary `crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/` → `Method::GovernedChange` with the actor stamped from the `VerifiedRequestContext`. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R031.1` | **The audit append accounts the serving write version.** After an audit append, the serving write version equals the authoritative scope version. Each attempt commits one maintenance batch under a unique attempt id. The kernel advances the authoritative version by one per committed batch, maintenance included. The served append accounts that commit in the serving projection. A governed ingest stamps its graph version expectation from the serving version. That write commits after the append. It never meets the +1 skew the kernel refuses as `STALE_VERSION`. Caller chain: served `Method::AuditAppend` → `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs:683` → `dispatch_helpers.rs` (line 697 accounts the serving version after every successful append, replay included). Shape: the accounting sits in `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/dispatch_helpers.rs`; it reads the authoritative scope version through `authoritative_graph_version` of `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/`. | One test exercises the served append. It performs one audit append, then the write stamped from the serving version. It asserts the serving and authoritative versions agree. It asserts the stamped write commits through the kernel version check. It asserts a replayed append advances the serving version by exactly one. After that, the two agree again. Test: `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/dispatch_helpers.rs::an_audit_append_accounts_the_serving_version_for_stamped_writes` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R031.2` | **Writes are stamped from the authoritative scope version.** A durable commit can advance a graph's mutation scope without the serving projection accounting it. The authoritative version read raises the serving write version to the durable one exactly once. This precedes any new version stamp. SourceIngest stamps its batch from that read. A fresh single-writer ingest after such a commit therefore commits. It never meets the kernel's `STALE_VERSION` refusal. A serving version ahead of its authority is still refused. Caller chain: a governed `SourceIngest` stamp → `authoritative_graph_version` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/`) → the persistence version read, raising the serving `GraphCore` version. Shape: the one function `authoritative_graph_version(persistence, graph, core)` in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/`; SourceIngest calls it before stamping. | Test `the_authoritative_version_accounts_a_durable_write_the_projection_missed` in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/tests.rs` puts the projection two versions behind the durable scope. The read returns the durable version and the projection equals it. A projection ahead of its authority refuses. Test: `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation_batch/tests.rs::the_authoritative_version_accounts_a_durable_write_the_projection_missed` (L2). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R031.3` | **AuditAppend carries bounded evidence into the chain entry.** `Method::AuditAppend` takes an optional `evidence` map of identifiers and counts (a screen verdict, finding classes, rule ids, hook decisions). It holds at most 16 keys, each key a token of at most 256 bytes, each value at most 256 bytes with no control character, `|` or `;`. A map outside those bounds refuses `AUDIT_FIELDS_INVALID` and writes nothing. The entry renders the sorted pairs as `evidence=k=v;k=v` in the chain line, and the map is part of the reservation-to-outcome equality, so an outcome that changes it does not close the reservation. An absent map leaves the entry and every older request body unchanged. Caller chain: served `Method::AuditAppend` → `native_routes.rs` → `OperationAuditEvent::validate` and `line` in `crates/medulla-store/src/redb_store/operation_audit.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/operation_audit_tests.rs::evidence_is_bounded_rendered_in_the_chain_line_and_part_of_the_outcome_equality` (L1); served proof `tests/test_audit_append_evidence_served.py::test_evidence_reaches_the_chain_entry_and_oversize_is_refused` (L3). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R035.5` | **Query cancellation stops a running statement.** Subsystem: wire listeners. Owner: the wire layer's `ConnectionManager` is the one registry of live connections, keyed by `(pid, secret_key)`. The engine's `CancelHandler` only receives the request and delegates; it holds no state. This mirrors the wire layer's own split, where `DefaultCancelHandler` wraps a `ConnectionManager`. Caller chain: `serve_prepared` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/mod.rs`) → `message_loop::serve_connection` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/message_loop.rs`) → `process_message` → `EngineCancelHandler::on_cancel_request` → `ConnectionManager::cancel`. Shape: `serve_prepared` builds one `Arc<ConnectionManager>` per listener and hands it to `EngineBackendFactory`. `EngineBackendFactory::cancel_handler` returns `EngineCancelHandler`, in new `crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/cancel.rs`. It replaces the default no-op handler. `on_cancel_request` calls `ConnectionManager::cancel(pid, &secret_key)` and logs a warning when the call returns `false`. So a wrong secret changes nothing. Registration: `complete_login` (`crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/auth.rs`) already sets the pid and secret from `RandomPidSecretKeyGenerator`. Its `finish_authentication` already sends `BackendKeyData`. `complete_login` then calls `ConnectionManager::register(pid, secret_key)`. It stores the returned `Arc<ConnectionHandle>` and `ConnectionGuard` in the client's `session_extensions`. The guard unregisters on disconnect. Per statement: both `do_query` handlers of `EngineBackend` take `ConnectionHandle::start_query()` before they run the statement. They pass a fresh `medulla_sql::sql::CancellationToken` to `WireSession::run_read`. It replaces the `CancellationToken::new()` literal there. When the receiver fires, the handler calls `CancellationToken::cancel` and returns SQLSTATE `57014`. The key belongs to one connection, so a cancel never crosses tenants. Matrix: the row adds `PgOperationalSurface::QueryCancellation` to the const `SERVED_OPERATIONAL_SURFACES` in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/pg_operational_surface.rs`. `PgCompatibilityMatrix::with_served_forms` declares every surface that const lists. The first R035 child to land creates the const. Clients read the surface through `EngineCapabilities` of MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R020.2, so the row writes no reporting code. Budget: the statement stops within 100 ms of the cancel. The registry adds under 256 bytes per connection. | Test: `tests/pgwire_roundtrip.rs::cancel_request_stops_a_long_query_and_wrong_secret_is_ignored` (L4, planned) runs a 5 s query and asserts `57014` under 500 ms. |

The class is visible and never silently downgraded. The loss window is exported. Authorization and HMAC-protected keyspace isolation apply in every class. Split per the net-new rule (no `DurabilityClass` type existed on main): `.1` is the typed model (this requirement's rollup is LANDED only once every child is.). | Verified by kill-point tests per class and a measurement test confirming the exported async loss window stays within the declared bound. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R038.3` | **A KV namespace declares its durability class, and the class is read back.** `Method::KvDeclareNamespace {namespace, class, shared}` stores the declaration as one native MutationBatch row of `kv.redb` (the durable path, commit before acknowledgment, replay by operation identity) and answers `KvNamespaceClass {class, declared, loss_window_ms}`. `Method::KvNamespaceDeclaration {namespace, shared}` reads it back; a namespace that never declared answers `sync` with `declared: false`. The class name parses through `DurabilityClass::parse_declared`; an unknown name is refused `DURABILITY_CLASS_UNKNOWN` and stores nothing. A redeclaration goes through the one rule `DurabilityClass::admit_redeclaration`: the same class is accepted, `async` may become `sync`, and every other change, in particular any change between `ephemeral` and a durable class, is refused `DURABILITY_CLASS_MISMATCH` naming the current and requested class. A namespace already holding acknowledged rows counts as `sync`, so it can never be declared `ephemeral`. A namespace declared `ephemeral` is served from process memory, never appends to the durable log, and loses its rows at restart while its declaration stays. A declaration is keyed by the served namespace key, which already carries the verified tenant and principal, so another owner never sees it. Caller chain: served `Method::KvDeclareNamespace` / `KvNamespaceDeclaration` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` → `dispatch_handlers::handle` → `kv_declare` / `kv_declaration` → `KvStore::declare_class` / `namespace_class` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/declaration.rs); every served read and write of the namespace consults `KvStore::ephemeral_rows`. Shape: `KvNamespaceClass` in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/storage_wire/kv.rs; the codes are `ServerErrorCode::DurabilityClassMismatch` and `DurabilityClassUnknown`; the declaration rows live under one reserved namespace of the existing KV table, so no layout changes. The TTL of an ephemeral namespace and the group-commit window of an `async` namespace are enforced by the rows that own them, not here. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R038.1, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R038.2, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102. | Tests: `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_namespace_declaration_reads_back_survives_restart_and_refuses_weakening` (L3); `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_ephemeral_namespace_is_served_from_memory_and_not_declared_over_durable_rows` (L3); `crates/medulla-types/src/durability_class.rs::redeclaration_never_weakens_and_never_crosses_ephemeral` (L1). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070` | **Operator command inspects and applies registered offline store upgrades.** The server binary has one operator command with two verbs over a data directory. `inspect` reads the format of every store and writes nothing. Its exit status says one of four things. Nothing needs doing, or an upgrade is available. A store may be in a format this build neither opens nor upgrades. A store may also be unreadable. `apply` requires an explicit confirmation flag and refuses while an engine holds the directory. It runs each registered offline upgrade in place and keeps the existing rows. It stops at the first failure and leaves that store in its previous layout. It never writes a store it does not upgrade. A second run reports nothing to do. The upgrades come from one registry in the storage kernel, so a new upgrade is one entry. With automatic migration off (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.1`), the engine refuses to start on a store that a registered upgrade applies to. The refusal carries the named format error of that store and the exact command to run. No other store state refuses startup this way. Any other failure of a lazily opened store stays local to that store. | Command tests run on disposable stores. Inspect on current stores reports nothing to do and writes nothing. Inspect on a registered predecessor reports an available upgrade. Apply upgrades graph-shard, Agent Library and SQL catalog predecessors and keeps the exact row bytes. A second apply is a no-op. Both verbs refuse while the engine lock of the directory is held. Apply without confirmation opens nothing. An undeclared or non-upgradable format is reported and stays byte-identical. A failed upgrade stops the run and leaves later stores untouched. Apply still upgrades a predecessor that an unclean shutdown left. The startup check and the ordinary store opens refuse a predecessor with the named error and the command. Storage-kernel tests prove that every registry entry is a declared predecessor. They also prove that each entry reaches the current layout exactly once. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.1` | **The engine migrates an older supported store when it opens it.** `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate.rs` runs the registered upgrades of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070` through `apply_store`. `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:155` calls it right after the engine takes its exclusive directory lock, before any store opens. The function takes the held lock as a parameter. It writes a journal under `<data-dir>/.store-upgrade-staging` before the first upgrade and replaces it atomically after each one. Each upgrade is one atomic commit, so an interrupted run leaves every store in one layout. The next open finds the journal, classifies every store again and resumes. A second run on current stores writes nothing. Each step is logged and counted in `medulla_store_migration_steps_total{step,outcome}`. A declared predecessor with no upgrade is refused as `STORE_FORMAT_TOO_OLD`. A layout outside the lineage is refused as `STORE_FORMAT_TOO_NEW`. The engine writes neither of them. A graph shard in either state stops startup; another store keeps its own lazy-open refusal. `MEDULLA_STORE_AUTO_MIGRATE` is on by default. `false` turns migration off, and an unknown value is refused as `STORE_AUTO_MIGRATE_INVALID_SWITCH`. Caller chain: server boot `crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:322` (switch) and `:331` (call, after the directory lock) → `migrate_on_open` (`crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate.rs:145`) → `apply_store`. Shape: `migrate_on_open` takes the held `PersistDirLock`; the journal lives under `<data-dir>/.store-upgrade-staging`; the metric is `medulla_store_migration_steps_total{step,outcome}`. The switch parser is `auto_migrate_enabled` of the store-upgrade module. No second runner exists. | `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs` tests disposable predecessor stores. Migration under the held lock keeps the row bytes and clears the journal. A second run writes nothing. A run stopped after one store leaves the journal, and the next open resumes it. With the switch off, the old named refusal and command stay. A too-new shard and a too-old blob store are refused by code and stay byte-identical. The metric samples render. Tests: `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs::open_migrates_a_previous_patch_store_under_the_held_lock_and_keeps_rows` (L2); `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/auto_migrate_tests.rs::an_interrupted_migration_resumes_at_the_next_open` (L2). |

This build opens it with no offline step and serves it. `ListGraphs` returns the commons and tenant graphs for the System identity. A second restart finds nothing to migrate. With the switch off, the engine exits with `GRAPH_SHARD_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` and the command, and `store-upgrade apply` still works. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.3` | **Persisted authority records keep the protocol id they were signed with.** Subsystem: durable kernel, store upgrade. Releases before 3.0 signed every authority record with protocol id `au-eg.query.v1`. The 3.0 rename changed the id to `au-medulla.query.v1`. The id is part of each record's canonical digest, so a stored record cannot be rewritten. A persisted authority context, signed envelope, operation replay identity and nonce replay key must validate under either id. One evidence aggregate must carry one id in all of its parts. A new write must carry only the current id: `AuthorityContext::validate_new` refuses any other id. The two minting constructors, `MutationBatch::for_compiled_batch` and `MutationRequestEnvelope::new`, call it. An unknown id must stay refused. No other persisted identifier changed in the rename. Domain-separation tags (`eg/...`), table names, schema ids and the `eg-body:sha256:` content prefix kept their bytes. Outbox topics, media types and the reasoner namespace changed, but no store-open path validates them. Caller chain: server boot → `migrate_on_open` (R070.1) → graph-shard upgrade inspection → mutation record decode → `AuthorityContext::validate` (`crates/medulla-identity-core-types/src/authority/context.rs`). Shape: `PRE_RENAME_AUTHORITY_PROTOCOL` and `is_recorded_authority_protocol` in that file; no new type and no rewrite of stored bytes. Budget: the check adds one string comparison per validated record, under 1 µs, and allocates nothing. The store-open cost is measured by group `cold_reopen_read` of `benches/cold_warm_reopen.rs`. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.1. | Tests: `crates/medulla-identity-core-types/src/authority/tests.rs::a_record_signed_under_the_pre_rename_protocol_still_validates` (L1); `crates/medulla-identity-core-types/src/authority/tests.rs::the_protocol_id_stays_bound_into_the_record_digests` (L1); `crates/medulla-identity-core-types/src/authority/tests.rs::a_new_write_accepts_only_the_current_protocol` (L1). Served proof: `tests/test_store_upgrade_from_production.py` (R070.4). |

Budget: the job finishes in under 10 minutes and the test holds under 2 GiB of resident memory; the job duration in CI is the measure. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R078` | **The mutation chokepoint stamps transaction time and checks the valid interval.** Subsystem: bitemporal facts. Caller chain: served `AddEdge`, `ApplyMutation`, `InvalidateEdge` and `SupersedeEdge` → `crates/medulla-server-api/src/mutation_apply.rs` → `GraphTxn` in `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/txn_edges.rs`. The gateway overwrites `tx_now` with engine time before the method is logged, so replay uses the logged value. An edge gets `tx_from = tx_now`. Invalidation sets `valid_until` and `tx_to`. A caller provides `valid_from`; it defaults to `tx_now`. No separate stamping method exists. An interval with `valid_until` before `valid_from` refuses with `VALIDITY_INVERTED`, registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Every stamp passes this single write chokepoint. The stamp types are the existing `NodeData` and `EdgeData` fields `valid_from`, `valid_until`, `tx_from` and `tx_to`, kept in MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R609.1. Shape: the stamping is in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/mutation_apply.rs` (the `tx_now` overwrite before logging) over `GraphTxn` edge methods in `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/txn_edges.rs`; `VALIDITY_INVERTED` is a new `ServerErrorCode` variant. | Test: `tests/bitemporal_served.rs::engine_stamps_tx_time_and_refuses_an_inverted_interval` (L3, planned) writes through signed dispatch, replays the log and compares stamps. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R079` | **As-of reads return the version valid and recorded at an instant.** Subsystem: bitemporal facts. Caller chain: served `Method::UnifiedQuery` with UQL `Op::AsOf { ts, axis }` → the as-of operator over `crates/medulla-epistemic/src/model.rs`. Axis `valid` keeps edges with `valid_from <= ts < valid_until`. Axis `tx` keeps edges with `tx_from <= ts < tx_to`. An open end counts as infinity. Both axes together answer what was believed and valid at two instants. `AS OF` filters edge visibility as well as node rows. The windows of an edge come from its `SupersedeEdge` history: each superseded version closes the previous version's `valid_until` and `tx_to` and opens the next version's `valid_from` and `tx_from`, and `live_at` reads those windows. A traversal follows only the edges valid at `ts`; a node reached only through an edge outside its window is not reached. Edge rows are emitted only when the query projects edges, and they pass the same window filter, so a projected edge is never one a traversal could not have followed. An edge with no `SupersedeEdge` history uses its own `valid_from`, `valid_until`, `tx_from` and `tx_to`. Shape: the as-of operator is `Op::AsOf { ts, axis }` of the UQL grammar (`crates/medulla-uql/src/grammar.rs:171`); the window predicate is `live_at` of `crates/medulla-epistemic/src/model.rs:151` (`live_at_valid` and `live_at_tx`), applied to node rows, to the edges a traversal follows and to projected edge rows, with the windows assembled from the `SupersedeEdge` history by one helper in the same file. | Test: `tests/uql_dag_served.rs::as_of_returns_the_right_version_at_two_instants_on_both_axes` (L3, planned) writes superseded versions through `SupersedeEdge` and asserts that a traversal at each instant follows only the edge valid then and that edge rows appear only when projected. Test: `crates/medulla-plan/src/tests.rs::as_of_returns_the_right_version_at_two_instants_on_both_axes` (L2, planned). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R082.7` | **A running mining job observes cancellation, deadline and CPU budget.** Subsystem: job plane. Caller chain: `worker_loop` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/executor_loop.rs:97`) → `execute_claim` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/executor_claim.rs:7`) → the status tick in its select loop (`executor_claim.rs:122`). While a kernel runs, the executor polls job status every `min(5000 ms, lease_ms / 3)` and renews the lease otherwise. A cancel request stops the kernel and the job ends `Cancelled`; the state itself is the reason, so no reason text is stored and `JobState::Cancelled` keeps its shape (no wire change). A passed deadline ends `Failed` with `deadline_exceeded`. An exhausted CPU budget ends `Failed` with `cpu_budget_exceeded`. No lease renewal happens after the stop. Shape: today the tick is a fixed 250 ms interval (`executor_claim.rs:87`) and the renewal passes a fixed 60 000 ms (`executor_claim.rs:150`). This row derives both from the claim's lease as `min(5000, lease_ms / 3)`. The stop reasons `deadline_exceeded` and `cpu_budget_exceeded` already exist at `executor_claim.rs:140`. The cancelled branch calls `mark_cancelled_fenced` and stays as it is. Reuse `JobStore::fail_attempt_fenced` (`crates/medulla-jobs/src/store.rs:1084`). | Test: `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/graph_tests.rs::cancel_deadline_and_cpu_budget_each_stop_the_kernel_with_their_reason` (L3) uses a long synthetic kernel for all three. |

That function sets `not_before_ms` from `RetryPolicy.backoff_ms` (`store.rs:1112`; `RetryPolicy` at `crates/medulla-jobs/src/model.rs:131`). Failure codes are the closed set `invalid_payload`, `kernel_failure`, `deadline_exceeded`, `cpu_budget_exceeded` and `worker_execution_failed`. A failure record holds no payload text, endpoint or identity. A failed attempt becomes claimable again only after `backoff_ms`. Attempts stop at `max_attempts`. Shape: the closed failure-code set becomes one `const` slice in `executor_claim.rs`. The codes appear today as literals at `executor_claim.rs:108` and `executor_loop.rs:137`. The `Failed` reason is exactly one of the five codes and never holds payload text. `kernel_cancelled` is not a failure code: `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/worker_publish.rs:230` stops accepting it from a remote worker (it refuses like any code outside the set, hard cutover), and the colocated kernel-error branch at `executor_program.rs:207` records `kernel_failure`. Code: add `pub fn resolve_write_conflict(conflict: &WriteConflict, strategy: MergeStrategy, current: &BTreeMap<String, Value>, incoming: &BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, Value>, WriteConflict>` to `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/write_conflict.rs`. The name differs from the query-planner symbol `resolve_conflict`. Rule: `Refuse` returns the conflict. `PreferNewer` returns `incoming`. `FieldUnion` keeps every key that only one side holds and every key with equal values on both sides. A key with different values on both sides returns the conflict. The function is pure and deterministic. Refusals: it returns the input `WriteConflict`; R084.4 maps it to a code. Shape: `WriteConflict` and `MergeStrategy` are in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/write_conflict.rs`. The new function is pure and sits beside them. The value type is the `BTreeMap<String, Value>` that `core.get_node_properties` decodes. Writes: none. Depends: R084.1. | Planned: `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/write_conflict.rs::tests::resolve_under_each_strategy` (L1) covers each strategy, one disjoint union and one colliding key. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R084.3` | **Wiring: version check calls the resolver on the served envelope path.** Stage: durable kernel, served `ApplyChangeEnvelope` and `ApplyChangeEnvelopes`. Caller chain: `dispatch_change_envelopes` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs:32`) → `apply_one_change_envelope_inner` (`change_envelope.rs:611`) → `prepare_change_envelope_commit` (`change_envelope.rs:752`) → `validate_change_envelope_version` (`change_envelope.rs:793`), then `resolve_envelope_conflict` (new). Add `resolve_envelope_conflict(envelope, conflict, strategy, core) -> Result<ChangeEnvelope, WriteConflict>`. The strategy comes from the new field `write_conflict_strategy` on the server state, set from `Args` in `src/main.rs`. Under `Refuse` the function returns the conflict at once. Under another strategy, every operation must be `Method::AddNode` on an existing node, else the conflict stands. For each such operation the function decodes the current properties with `core.get_node_properties`, calls `resolve_write_conflict` and replaces `properties_msgpack`. It then sets `VersionExpectation::Graph(actual)` and calls `reseal_envelope`. A known replay skips the check, as today. The kernel check `STALE_VERSION` in `crates/medulla-transaction/src/commit.rs::admit_fresh` stays the last guard. Shape: `reseal_envelope` is `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/mutation_batch/model/batch.rs:215`. `GraphCore::get_node_properties` is `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_readthrough.rs:11`. The strategy field lives in the server state built from `ServerArgs`. Writes: the existing envelope commit only. The Rust `ServerErrorCode` is the only source, and the hand-written client follows it (`MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R001.4`). Rule: under `Refuse` the response is `WRITE_CONFLICT: expected {e}, actual {a}, scope {s}`. Under a merge strategy, two cases return `WRITE_CONFLICT_UNMERGEABLE` with the same three values. One is an operation that is not `AddNode` on an existing node. The other is a field collision. Both refusals change no graph state, no version and no outbox row. Evidence: R084.5. Types: `MemorySnapshotHead { doc_id, revision: u64, parent_revision: Option<u64>, content_digest: Digest256, content_len: u32, graph_incarnation: String, provenance: SnapshotProvenance, retain_until_ms: Option<u64> }`; `SnapshotProvenance { actor, policy_digest, request_id }`; `SyncAction { ObserveFile, Submit, Resolve, Acknowledge }`; `SyncRequest { request_id, doc_id, action }` with per-action fields; `SyncState { InSync, Pending, Conflict, Unavailable }`; `ListCursor(String)`. `SyncOutcome` is tagged: `Accepted { head }`, `ConflictRetained { head, candidate_revision }`, `Resolved { head }`, `Observed { state, head_revision, head_digest }`, `Acknowledged { state }` and `Refused { code }`. `Refused` holds no head field. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.2` | **Behavior: versioned reads and stable listing.** Stage: durable kernel, served `ReadMemorySnapshot` and `ListMemorySnapshots`. Rule: `ReadMemorySnapshot { doc_id, revision: Option<u64> }` returns the head or the named retained revision with its bytes. `ListMemorySnapshots { prefix, limit, cursor }` returns heads in `doc_id` order. `limit` defaults to 50 and has a maximum of 200. The cursor is a signed token over the last `doc_id`, the tenant, graph incarnation, principal, policy digest and filter digest. It uses HMAC-SHA256 under the server auth secret. Config `memory_sync.cursor_ttl_s`, default 900, bounds [60, 86400], in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs::MemorySyncConfig`. A list never reveals hidden counts, identifiers or digests. Refusals: `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_INVALID`, `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_EXPIRED`, `MEMSYNC_NOT_AVAILABLE`. Shape: the read arm extends `try_handle_memory_maintenance` in `memory.rs:18`. The cursor starts from `CursorFamily::encode` and `decode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/tenant_cursor.rs:32` and `:45`). The HMAC signature follows the `Hmac<Sha256>` use in `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/auth.rs`. Writes: none. Depends: R085.1. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::authorized_read_list_and_cursor_isolation` (L3) asserts paging, a cursor reused by another principal, an expired cursor and a hidden document, plus one cross-tenant negative case. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.3` | **Behavior: sync decisions and compare-and-set.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot`. Caller chain: `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:166`) → `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/memory_snapshot.rs::decide_sync` (new). Pure core: `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs::decide(head, request) -> Decision`. `ObserveFile` compares the observed digest with the head and answers `Observed`; it writes nothing. `Submit { expected_revision: Option<u64>, content, content_digest }` accepts when `expected_revision` equals the head revision, or the document is absent and `expected_revision` is `None`. The new revision is the head revision plus 1 and its parent is the old head. A submit with the head digest returns `Accepted` with no new revision. A stale submit keeps its content as a retained candidate, leaves the head unchanged and returns `ConflictRetained`. `Resolve { candidate_revision, expected_head_revision, resolution }` has `KeepHead`, `TakeCandidate` or `Merged { content, content_digest }`. It applies only when `expected_head_revision` equals the head and the candidate is retained. `Acknowledge` is R085.6. A repeated `request_id` returns the stored outcome for `memory_sync.receipt_ttl_s`, default 86400, bounds [3600, 2592000]. Shape: the pure core is `decide` in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The write path reuses `commit_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest/graph.rs:15`) as `try_handle_memory` does (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:315`). Writes: through R085.4 only. Depends: R085.1. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::competing_saves_retain_both_and_resolve_once` (L3) saves twice against one head, asserts both versions stay, resolves once and replays a request id. |

Code: add the three `Method` variants to the last chunk file `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_11.rs`. Register them as rows in `ROWS` of `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/graph.rs` with scopes `memory:read` and `memory:write`, in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation/plan.rs`, and in `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access/write_classification.rs`. The Rust types are the only source, and the Python client is hand-written. The sync handler compiles one `MutationBatch` with `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/compile.rs::compile_methods_with_outbox`. The batch holds the head node `memsnap:<doc_id>` and the revision node. It also holds the blob holder row in namespace `memory_snapshot` and one outbox intent `memory_snapshot_synced`. A submit that changes existing content calls `require_admit_receipts` (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R141.104`) before the commit. No direct `GraphCore` write bypasses the gateway. The router plans the rows (`SnapshotPlan` is internal and never a field of a served method) and commits them through the engine-internal method `SyncMemorySnapshotStore { request, plan }`, which a caller may not name; the receipt digests that admit a change are `SyncRequest.admit_receipt_digests`. Shape: `compile_methods_with_outbox` is `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/compile.rs:140`. The scopes exist at `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/scopes.rs:170`. The manifest test that walks the chunk files is `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/contract_registry.rs`. The blob holder namespace is a string newtype, `HolderNamespace::new("memory_snapshot")` (`crates/medulla-server-blob/src/blob/store/holders.rs:80`). Holder ordering and identity: the content is first put into the content-addressed store (`put_chunk`, idempotent) BEFORE the graph commit, so a storage failure refuses `MEMSYNC_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` before anything commits. The holder is always registered under the document owner's scope as holder `<doc_id>@<revision>` for a revision and `<doc_id>@c<n>` for retained candidate `n` (`SnapshotSlot::holder_id`), in namespace `memory_snapshot`, through a blob-domain batch; the router and the consumer produce the same holder row, so registration is idempotent. A holder registration that fails after the graph commit returns success with a receipt warning (`SyncReceipt.warnings` holds `MEMSYNC_HOLDER_PENDING`; `SyncReceipt` flattens the `SyncOutcome` and adds `warnings`, absent when empty), never a failure for a committed write, and the consumer repairs it. The `memory_snapshot_synced` consumer is a service actor `engine:memory-snapshot` in `crates/medulla-server/src`, built with the persistence outbox API (`subscribe_mutation_outbox`, `claim_mutation_outbox`, `ack_mutation_outbox`) and copying the claim, ack loop of an existing outbox consumer (`repository_enrichment_worker`); it acts with delegated authority but writes the holder in the owner's scope. Writes: the gateway commit only. Depends: R085.1, R085.3, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R141.104`. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::wire_methods_reach_durable_gateway` (L3) asserts that head, revision, holder row and outbox row commit in one batch and that the manifest lists three methods. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.5` | **Refusals: authority before lookup, redaction and no effects.** Stage: durable kernel, served memory methods. Caller chain: as R085.4, then the authority check in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/memory_snapshot.rs` (new) before any lookup. Code: `MemorySyncRefusal` codes in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`, registered in `ServerErrorCode`. The handler derives tenant, principal, graph and policy from the verified request context. It checks `memory:read` or `memory:write` before any lookup, cursor use or acknowledgment. Codes: `MEMSYNC_NOT_AVAILABLE` (denied existing and absent documents, including creation attempts), `MEMSYNC_VERSION_MALFORMED`, `MEMSYNC_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, `MEMSYNC_CONTENT_TOO_LARGE`, `MEMSYNC_CONTENT_MALFORMED`, `MEMSYNC_INCARNATION_EXPIRED`, `MEMSYNC_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE`, `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_INVALID`, `MEMSYNC_CURSOR_EXPIRED`. A refusal holds no head metadata and changes no durable record or holder. Shape: `ServerErrorCode` is `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`. `MemorySyncRefusal` is new in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. Scope checks reuse `crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access/write_classification.rs`. Writes: none. Depends: R085.4. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::denied_and_malformed_requests_have_no_effects` (L3) asserts each code, identical bytes for denied and absent, and unchanged records, plus one cross-tenant negative case. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.6` | **Evidence: provenance and materialization acknowledgment.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot` `Acknowledge` and the `Observed` outcome. Caller chain: as R085.4, then `decide_sync` (R085.3) in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/memory_snapshot.rs` (new) for the `Acknowledge` action. Code: `Acknowledge { doc_id, revision, content_digest, observation_generation: u64 }` in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The handler stores the acknowledgment as a record separate from the accepted content. Rule: state is `InSync` only when three values match. The acknowledged revision, digest and generation must equal the head revision, the head digest and the latest observation generation. No unacknowledged submit or unresolved conflict may exist. A pending edit or conflict gives `Pending` or `Conflict`. Missing evidence gives `Unavailable`. Each revision persists actor, policy digest, request id, parent revision, content digest and outcome. Shape: `Acknowledge` and `SyncState` are new in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. The acknowledgment record is a node `memack:<doc_id>` in the same `MutationBatch` as R085.4. Writes: the R085.4 gateway commit. Depends: R085.3, R085.4. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::provenance_and_materialization_ack_survive_restart` (L3) asserts each state, the stored provenance and the same state after an engine restart. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.7` | **Retention: conflicts and revisions survive restart and collection.** Stage: durable kernel, served `SyncMemorySnapshot`. Caller chain: as R085.4 for writes; the existing blob collector in `crates/medulla-server-blob/src/blob/store/gc.rs` for release. Code: `HolderNamespace` is a validated string newtype (`crates/medulla-server-blob/src/blob/store/holders.rs:80`), so the namespace is `HolderNamespace::new("memory_snapshot")` and no enum variant is added. Each revision holds its blob as holder `<doc_id>@<revision>` and each retained candidate as `<doc_id>@c<n>`, so a revision and a candidate with the same number never share a holder. Rule: an unresolved conflict never expires. A resolved candidate and a superseded revision release after `memory_sync.history_retention_s`, default 2592000, bounds [3600, 31536000]. A document keeps at most `memory_sync.max_revisions_per_doc` revisions, default 64, bounds [1, 4096]. A tenant holds at most `memory_sync.capacity_bytes`, default 1073741824, bounds [1048576, 1099511627776]. A write over capacity returns `MEMSYNC_RETENTION_EXHAUSTED` before any effect. Release needs an authorized policy and a passed deadline. The three limits are knobs registered in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs` beside `memory_sync.cursor_ttl_s`: `memory_sync.history_retention_s` (U64, 2592000, [3600, 31536000], "seconds", Tunable), `memory_sync.max_revisions_per_doc` (U64, 64, [1, 4096], "revisions", Tunable) and `memory_sync.capacity_bytes` (U64, 1073741824, [1048576, 1099511627776], "bytes", Tunable). `MemorySyncConfig::from_knobs()` reads them; no `Args` flag exists. The consumer releases holders on a tick: it calls `HolderChange::release` for each revision past the retention deadline or beyond the revision cap (oldest first), never for an unresolved conflict. L1 `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs::tests::memory_sync_retention_knobs_register` asserts the three names, defaults and bounds. Shape: holders use `HolderChange::acquire` and `release` (`holders.rs:113` and `:125`) and `HolderReconcile` (`holders.rs:162`). The handler crate `medulla-h-graph` gains a `medulla-server-blob` dependency, as `medulla-h-admin` has in `connector_pack/import.rs`. `MemorySyncConfig` is new in `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/memory_sync.rs`. Depends: R085.4. | Planned: `tests/memory_sync_served.rs::conflicts_survive_gc_and_retention_refusal` (L3) runs a collector pass and a restart, then asserts retained candidates and the exhaustion refusal. |

Code: new `crates/medulla-work-types/src/agent_bus.rs` holds `BusParticipant { session_ref, tenant, host_ref, capabilities: BTreeSet<String>, last_seen_ms: u64 }`. Topic messages use `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R850` (`topic.append`, `topic.read`). Hub peers use `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R851`. Medulla keeps no second message model. Bounds: `session_ref` has at most 128 bytes. A participant has at most 64 capabilities of 64 bytes each. A tenant has at most 10000 participants (`Fixed`). Caller chain: `BusRegister`, `BusHeartbeat` and `BusRoster` handlers in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/bus.rs` (new) → `BusParticipant` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/agent_bus.rs` (new). Shape: `BusParticipant` and `HubPeer` exist today in `crates/medulla-durable/src/agent_bus.rs` (re-exported at `crates/medulla-durable/src/lib.rs:89`, module at `lib.rs:77`). This row moves the participant type to `medulla-types`, deletes that file and `crates/medulla-durable/tests/agent_bus.rs`, and removes the two `lib.rs` lines. Writes: none. Depends: R850, R851. | Planned: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/agent_bus.rs::tests::participant_round_trips_and_bounds` (L1). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R095.1001` | **Behavior: register, heartbeat and roster.** Stage: durable kernel, served bus methods. Caller chain: authenticated `dispatch` → `dispatch_graph_op` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline.rs:68`) → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs:166`) → `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/bus.rs::register`, `heartbeat`, `roster` (new). Rule: a participant is the node `bus:participant:<session_ref>` with label `BusParticipant` in the request graph. `BusRegister` replaces the node. `BusHeartbeat` sets `last_seen_ms` to the request clock in one property upsert, O(1). `BusRoster` scans the tenant's participants in O(n) and marks each `live` when `now - last_seen_ms` is at most `bus.participant_ttl_s`. Config `bus.participant_ttl_s`, default 90, bounds [10, 3600], set by `--bus-participant-ttl-s` in `ServerArgs` (`crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:24`, flattened into `src/main.rs::Args`). Dispatch and message transfer are not medulla behaviours: see ARBORA-OPS-R882. Shape: the node upsert reuses `commit_gateway` (`crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest/graph.rs:15`) as `try_handle_memory` does (`gateway_graph_routes.rs:315`). A new `is_bus` matcher joins `ROUTE_MATCHERS` (`gateway_graph_routes.rs:128`). Writes: the gateway commit only. Depends: R095.1000. | Planned: `tests/agent_bus_served.rs::register_heartbeat_and_roster_mark_liveness` (L3) registers two sessions, ages one past the ttl and asserts `live` flags. |

Register them as rows in `ROWS` of `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/graph.rs` with scope `bus:write` or `bus:read`, and in `crates/medulla-server-mutation/src/mutation/plan.rs`. Code: the codes are new variants in `ServerErrorCode` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`). `BUS_SCOPE_REQUIRED` when the caller lacks `bus:write` or `bus:read`. `BUS_SESSION_FOREIGN` when a heartbeat names a session registered by another principal. `BUS_ROSTER_FULL` at 10000 participants. `BUS_FIELD_TOO_LARGE` over the bounds of R095.1000. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1` | **Run checkpoint binds short-term and long-term memory references.** Subsystem: work items. Caller chain: served `Method::PublishRunCheckpoint` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs:40`) → `commit_verified_work_item` (`work_item.rs:104`) → the MutationBatch apply in `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:415` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::publish` (`checkpoint.rs:149`). The request already carries `short_term` and `long_term` in `CheckpointContent`, each a list of `{document_id, revision, digest}`. `publish` reads every referenced document through the R085 snapshot store and refuses a missing document or a digest mismatch. The checkpoint pointer and its R085 snapshot commit in one `MutationBatch`. Its expected-version precondition carries the WorkItem lease epoch and fencing token. Short-term memory keeps at most 64 revisions per run and expires when the WorkItem completes. Long-term memory follows R085 retention. Refusal codes: `CHECKPOINT_STALE_REVISION`, `CHECKPOINT_FENCE_STALE`, `CHECKPOINT_MEMORY_REF_MISSING`, `CHECKPOINT_DIGEST_MISMATCH`. Registered in `ServerErrorCode`. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1.2.1` | **Checkpoint revision retention bound.** Subsystem: work items. A short-term revision is one immutable checkpoint row of a WorkItem run in `RUN_CHECKPOINTS` (`checkpoint.rs`). Its key is the `checkpoint:<sha256>` reference that `publish` mints. The WorkItem head (`WORK_ITEM_CHECKPOINT_REF`, `WORK_ITEM_CHECKPOINT_REVISION`, `WORK_ITEM_CHECKPOINT_DIGEST`) always names the newest revision. Caller chain: served `Method::PublishRunCheckpoint` → `try_handle` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item.rs`) → `commit_verified_work_item` → the MutationBatch apply in `crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint.rs::publish`. Shape: the WorkItem row carries a new property `WORK_ITEM_CHECKPOINT_HISTORY`, declared in `work_item_read` beside `WORK_ITEM_CHECKPOINT_REF`. It lists the references of the retained revisions, oldest first. `publish` appends the new reference in its write transaction. When the list then holds more than `SHORT_TERM_MAX_REVISIONS` entries, the same transaction removes the oldest reference and deletes its `RUN_CHECKPOINTS` row. The head is always the last entry, so pruning never deletes the head. A refused publish deletes nothing, because the prune shares its transaction. Completion through `CommitWorkItemResult` deletes no revision. The retained window stays readable after completion, as the landed test `completed_history_survives_claim_expiry_but_resume_requires_live_fence` requires. Memory documents that a revision references (MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085) are not deleted here. Their retention is `MemorySnapshotHead.retain_until_ms`, so the row needs no per-run memory link. Refusal: a history entry whose `RUN_CHECKPOINTS` row is absent refuses with the existing `checkpoint snapshot is missing` message of `publish`. Constant: `SHORT_TERM_MAX_REVISIONS: usize = 64`, a fixed `const` in `checkpoint.rs`. Budget: the prune adds at most one row delete per publish. The history adds at most 64 references, under 8 KiB, to the WorkItem row. The L2 test measures both. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.1. | Planned: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/checkpoint/tests.rs::sixty_fifth_revision_prunes_the_oldest_and_keeps_the_head` (L2) publishes 65 revisions. It asserts 64 rows remain, revision 1 is gone, the head reads back and completion deletes nothing. `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::retention_is_enforced_through_served_publish` (L3). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043.7` | **Attested envelope text admits `@` and `\` and refuses only host identity.** `ChangeEnvelope::validate` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs` screens caller-attested material values with the attested rule. These values are graph operation properties, compare-and-set conditions and updates, outbox payloads, evidence loci and feature values. The attested rule is in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs`. The rule SHALL admit ordinary document text that contains `@` or `\`. Examples are decorators, handles, version pins, LaTeX, escapes and Windows program paths. The rule SHALL refuse a host path that starts anywhere in the text. The host-path prefixes are the home, users, mnt and root prefixes in `HOST_PATH_PREFIXES`. The rule SHALL also refuse a local file URI, a Windows profile path (`<drive>:\Users\`) and an e-mail address. Property keys and envelope identifiers keep the strict character rule. Repository-snapshot material keeps its own rule. Caller chain: served `ApplyChangeEnvelope` → `apply_one_change_envelope_inner` (`crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/change_envelope.rs:611`) → the commit in `commit_change_envelope_with_budget` (`crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_mutation/change_envelope.rs:35`) → `ChangeEnvelope::validate` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope.rs:252`) → `validate_attested_text` (`crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs:105`). Shape: the rule is `HOST_PATH_PREFIXES` (`material.rs:56`) with `has_host_path_anywhere` (`material.rs:83`), `has_windows_profile_path` (`material.rs:94`) and `has_email` (`material.rs:77`). Repository-snapshot text keeps `validate_repository_text` (`material.rs:119`). | Tests: `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs::tests::attested_material_admits_ordinary_document_text` (L1) and `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/change_envelope/material.rs::tests::attested_material_still_refuses_host_identity` (L1). `crates/medulla-server-served-tests/src/tests.rs::test_served_change_envelope_admits_document_text_and_codes_refusals` (L3) commits document text with `@` and `\`. It sends `ApplyChangeEnvelopeDraft`, which continues as `ApplyChangeEnvelope`. |
