| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.2` | **Read the committed terminal event through the existing WorkItem outcome read.** `GetWorkItemOutcome` retains `work:read`, verified tenant and graph authorization. The fenced terminal CAS binds the engine-derived outbox batch and stored event byte digest to the WorkItem. One snapshot resolves that binding to the exact committed run-event row and exposes its scope, commit sequence, ordinal and safe result references. Legacy rows return explicit `legacy_unbound` unavailability; a missing or corrupt bound event refuses. No lease authority is projected. This point read excludes status/step history and lifecycle streaming. | Commit a real terminal extension, read its event, reopen storage and read the identical identity. Foreign tenants see nothing. Corrupt/missing bound events refuse. Legacy rows report unavailability. Generic writes cannot forge the native binding. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.2.1` | **Every WorkItem state transition is appended to a transition ledger, and a served read pages it.** Subsystem: WorkItem coordination. Class: BUILD, its own lane with a built engine (the lane measures the two layout digests below by running the lineage test; no digest is computed by hand and none is copied from this text). The ledger does not exist and this row adds it. (1) Ledger table: `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS: TableDefinition<(&str, &str, u64), &[u8]> = TableDefinition::new("work_item_transitions")` in `crates/medulla-store/src/redb_store/store_types.rs` beside `NODES`, keyed `(graph, work_item_id, lifecycle_revision)`; the value is the sealed (`DurableCrypto::seal`) MessagePack of new `WorkItemTransitionEntry { state: WorkItemStatus, at_ms: u64, revision: u64 }` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read.rs` (`state` is the row's status after the write, serialized as its snake_case stored text, `at_ms` the row's `updated_at` seconds times 1 000, `revision` the row's `row_revision` after the write). (2) Write: `write_work_item_props` (`crates/medulla-store/src/redb_store/store_read.rs`), the one writer of every WorkItem row, gains the parameter `transitions: &mut ScopedOwnerTableMut<'_, (&'static str, &'static str, u64), &'static [u8]>` and inserts one entry in the same transaction when the row is new (`lifecycle_revision` 0) or `transitioned` is true (the comparison it already makes for `lifecycle_revision`); a write that keeps the status appends nothing. `GraphRowTables` and `GraphRowTablesRef` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/graph_row_tables.rs`) gain `work_item_transitions`, opened with `member.open_scoped_table(WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS)`, and every caller of `write_work_item_props` passes it. Entries are never rewritten or deleted while the row lives; a removed WorkItem row removes its entries in the same transaction. (3) Layout: `work_item_transitions` joins the GraphShard census (`GRAPH_SHARD_TABLES` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/graph_shard.rs`) and a new predecessor `GRAPH_SHARD_BEFORE_WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` (the current census, its digest, `file_name: "graph-*.redb"`) is added to `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` (its frozen digest is `pinned_layout_digest(OwnerLayout::GraphShard)` read from the parent commit before the census change, and its table list is the parent census, as the sibling constants there do; the new pinned digest is the value `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage_tests.rs::every_layout_digest_is_pinned` reports after the change; if MEDULLA-PLACEMENT-R781.4 landed first, the parent census is the one after it and its predecessor stays in the list) and to the `offline_upgrade!` list of `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/offline_upgrade.rs`, the same pattern as `GRAPH_SHARD_BEFORE_DECISION_CERTIFICATES`; the census test count in `crates/medulla-store-apply-tests/src/shard_control_tests.rs` rises by one. A shard at the old layout refuses to open with `GRAPH_SHARD_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED`; the offline upgrade creates the empty table, so history starts at the first write after the upgrade. (4) Read: new `Method::ListWorkItemTransitions { tenant: String, work_item_id: String, #[serde(default)] cursor: Option<String>, #[serde(default = "default_transition_page")] limit: u32 }` (default 100, range [1, 200], outside refuses `INVALID_PARAMS`) beside `GetWorkItem` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_11.rs`, policy row in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/coordination.rs` with `authz_action` `work:read`, `mutates: false`. Result: `Option<WorkItemTransitionPage { entries: Vec<WorkItemTransitionEntry>, next_cursor: Option<String> }>`, entries in ascending `lifecycle_revision` (commit order); `next_cursor` is the decimal `lifecycle_revision` of the last entry when more exist. A WorkItem that is absent or not of the verified tenant answers `null`, exactly as `GetWorkItem` does, so a cross-tenant read learns nothing. The read writes nothing. Arbora consumer: `ARBORA-A2A-R011`. Python client: a hand-written `client.work_items.list_transitions(*, tenant, work_item_id, cursor=None, limit=100)`; it carries `tenant` because the wire method does and every sibling read passes it. Depends: none. Caller chain: served `Method::ListWorkItemTransitions` (new, beside `GetWorkItem` in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_11.rs) → a new arm beside `Method::GetWorkItem` in `crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs` building `WorkItemRead::Transitions { tenant, work_item_id, cursor, limit }` → `medulla_h_jobs::work_item_read::answer` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/work_item_read.rs`; the variant joins `tenant()` and answers `ResultPayload::of::<ListWorkItemTransitions>`, a new `result_contract::coordination` entry `=> Raw<Option<WorkItemTransitionPage>>`) → new `RedbBackend::list_work_item_transitions(graph_fname, tenant, work_item_id, cursor, limit)` beside `read_work_item` in `crates/medulla-persistence/src/redb_backend/work_item_read.rs` → new `list_work_item_transitions(shard, graph, tenant, work_item_id, cursor, limit, crypto) -> Result<Option<WorkItemTransitionPage>, String>` beside `read_work_item` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/read.rs`: `validate_work_item_get`, then `snapshot_row` and `WorkItemView::from_tenant_row` for the tenant check (`None` answers `None`), then in the same read snapshot a range scan of `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` over `(graph, work_item_id, start..)` taking `limit + 1` entries. Contracts and wire vectors regenerate. | Planned: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/read/tests.rs::transition_ledger_appends_on_status_change_only` (L2): submit (no dependencies), claim, renew twice (the first renewal starts the work) and commit one item; the ledger holds exactly the entries `ready`, `leased`, `running`, `succeeded` (the second renewal adds none) with ascending revisions; `limit 2` pages with `next_cursor`; another tenant reads `None`. Planned: `tests/work_items/test_transition_history_served.py::test_history_matches_ledger_in_commit_order` (L4) asserts the same order and paging through the served method and the client. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.3` | **Fenced metadata CAS requires a live worker lease.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::CasWorkItemMetadata` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease.rs`. When the request carries `expected_lease`, the transaction compares the stored lease expiry with the engine mutation timestamp. The call refuses when the timestamp is at or after expiry, or when expiry is absent, with `CONFLICT: lease not live`. Caller time never extends the fence. A request without `expected_lease` keeps the unfenced behavior. Rows stay unchanged on refusal. Shape: the lease clause is the `expected_lease` arm of `cas_work_item_metadata_preconditions_ok`, which compares `lease_expires_at` with `authoritative_now_ms` (the engine commit clock, never a request field); `validate_cas_work_item_metadata_request` (lease.rs:198) stays the shape check; tests live in crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease/tests.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/lease/tests.rs::fenced_cas_before_exact_and_after_expiry` (L2) covers the three instants and absent expiry. `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::fenced_cas_ignores_caller_clock` (L3) sends a future caller clock and asserts refusal. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101` | **Downtime work is an ontology that drives the engine scheduler (rollup).** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. The downtime ontology declares job types, priorities, budgets, windows and preconditions. The scheduler reads the ontology and enqueues eligible downtime jobs in each window. It binds every child. |

Declare `dw:JobType`, `dw:Window`, `dw:ResourceBudget`, `dw:Precondition` and `dw:priority`. The field `intent` names a registered `JobIntent` of `medulla-jobs`. A higher `priority` runs first. `budget` reuses `ResourceBudget` at `crates/medulla-jobs/src/model.rs:57` and must set `cpu_ms` and `memory_bytes`. A job type has at most 8 preconditions of at most 4 KiB each. `WindowKind` is `idle` or `declared`. The shapes file refuses a `dw:JobType` without `dw:ResourceBudget`. Caller chain: startup registration in `server_startup` (crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs) → `medulla_jobs::downtime::DowntimeJobType` validation → shapes check of the `core:downtime-work@1` source loaded by `current_core_catalog` (crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs). Shape: new files crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs (types `DowntimeJobType`, `DowntimeWindow`, `WindowKind`) and crates/medulla-core/ontology/downtime_work-v1.ttl plus `downtime_work-v1.shapes.ttl`; reuse `ResourceBudget` (crates/medulla-jobs/src/model.rs:57) and the intent registry in crates/medulla-jobs/src/intent.rs; the module is declared in crates/medulla-jobs/src/lib.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs::tests::job_type_round_trips` (L1) round-trips every field and refuses a job type without a budget. Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Code: `medulla_jobs::downtime::select_for_window(types, window, last_run, eval, ctx) -> DowntimeSelection`, pure, with no input or output. The `eval` argument is a `&dyn PreconditionEval`; `ctx` is a `WindowContext` carrying the clock reading, the foreground-activity reading, the window budget and the two idle knob values. An idle window opens when the foreground queue is empty and requests stay under `knob downtime.idle_max_rps` for `knob downtime.idle_quiet_secs`. A declared window opens at its start time. The rule `priority_then_budget_fit` orders eligible types by priority, then by oldest last run, then by IRI. It selects types while the summed budgets fit the window budget. A false precondition skips the type. Knobs: `knob downtime.idle_max_rps` default 1, [0, 100], requests per second. `knob downtime.idle_quiet_secs` default 60, [10, 3600], seconds. `knob downtime.window_cpu_permille` default 250, [10, 1000]. `knob downtime.window_memory_bytes` default 2 GiB, [256 MiB, 64 GiB]. Selection costs O(t log t) for t types. Caller chain: `DowntimeTick` in crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs `run_tick` → `medulla_jobs::downtime::select_for_window`. Shape: pure function and the `PreconditionEval` trait in crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs; it takes the window budget and the foreground-activity reading as plain arguments so it needs no server crate; the sort key is (priority desc, last_run asc, IRI asc) with the `cpu_ms` and `memory_bytes` of each `ResourceBudget` summed separately against the window budget. | Test: `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs::tests::priority_then_budget_fit` (L1) runs three types in priority order and skips one with a false precondition. Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Caller: `spawn_periodic_sweep(knob downtime.tick_secs, "downtime_scheduler", ..)` in `src/main.rs` → `server_startup::downtime_tick` → new `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::run_tick`. `knob downtime.tick_secs` is 1, [1, 60], seconds. `run_tick` reads `dw:JobType` instances from the commons graph with a SPARQL `SELECT`. It evaluates each precondition with `medulla_rdf::sparql::execute` and accepts `QueryOutcome::Boolean(true)`. Each chosen type submits one `JobStore::submit` for its intent under a `LeasePriority::BackgroundIngestion` lease from `AcquireCapacity`. The lease takes the type's `cpu_ms` from capacity cell `downtime:cpu` and its `memory_bytes` from cell `downtime:memory`, valid for 60 000 ms; the operator provisions both cells, and a type whose lease is not granted submits nothing. A type holding an unexpired lease is not selected again. The jobs carry a placement capability that only a downtime worker names, so analytics workers never lease them. A job type reads `dw:intent`, `dw:priority`, `dw:windowKind` (`idle` or `declared`), optional `dw:windowStartMs` and `dw:windowEndMs`, a `dw:hasBudget` node with `dw:cpuMs` and `dw:memoryBytes`, and `dw:hasPrecondition` nodes with a `dw:query` ASK text. The shipped intents `plan_evolution` and `live_artifact_refresh` register at the first tick. The window budget of one tick is `downtime.window_cpu_permille` times `downtime.tick_secs` in `cpu_ms` and `downtime.window_memory_bytes`. `DowntimeSelections` answers the latest tick: the types submitted with their job ids, and the types skipped for a precondition, a budget or a lease. The registered downtime job types include `plan_evolution` and `live_artifact_refresh`. Register the ontology in `crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs` as `core:downtime-work@1`. Add `DowntimeSelections` to `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/coordination.rs` with scope `jobs:read`. `run_tick` reads `ForegroundActivity`, which `.6` defines. Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.2`, `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R076`. Caller chain: `spawn_periodic_sweep` (src/main.rs:800), armed beside the `sealed_record_expiry` sweep (src/main.rs:1843) → `server_startup::downtime_tick` (new, beside `sealed_expiry_tick`, crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs:930) → `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::run_tick` → the jobs seam `JobsApi` (crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest.rs:43, installed with `JobsSeam` at seams_install.rs:26) → `JobStore::submit` (crates/medulla-jobs/src/store.rs:586). Shape: `run_tick` lives in medulla-server because it joins the SPARQL read (`medulla_rdf::sparql::execute`), the capacity lease and the jobs seam; the handler crates are not called directly. Add `DowntimeSelections` to crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/coordination.rs and the ontology source to the catalog in crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/schema_sources.rs; lease type `LeasePriority::BackgroundIngestion` is in crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/capacity_lease.rs:103. | Test: `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::tests::tick_enqueues_leased_jobs` (L3 through the real tick function with tenant context) asserts submitted jobs hold background leases. Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.4` | **A job type without a budget is skipped with a typed reason, and downtime jobs never reach a foreground worker.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Build row. Caller chain: `run_tick` (`crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs`) → `plan_from_graph` → `types_from_rows` → `DowntimeJobType::validate` (`crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs`). (1) Budget required: `TYPES_QUERY` in `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs` makes the budget pattern optional (`OPTIONAL { ?t dw:hasBudget ?b . ?b dw:cpuMs ?cpu ; dw:memoryBytes ?mem }`), and `types_from_rows` builds a type whose `budget.cpu_ms`/`budget.memory_bytes` are `None` when a value is absent instead of dropping the row. `DowntimeJobType::validate` returns `Result<(), DowntimeTypeRefusal>`, a new `Copy` enum in `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs` with variants `NoIri`, `IntentNotRegistered`, `BudgetRequired`, `TooManyPreconditions`, `PreconditionTooLarge` (each with a `Display` message equal to today's string). `plan_from_graph` returns the refused types beside the plan as `Vec<(String, DowntimeTypeRefusal)>`; a `BudgetRequired` type is recorded in the tick's selection as skipped with `DowntimeSkipReason::BudgetRequired` (R101.5), and no job, lease or capacity read happens for it; the other refusals stay a `tracing::warn!` and are not recorded. (2) Foreground isolation: a downtime job carries the placement capability `opaque_worker_capability("downtime")` (`DOWNTIME_CAPABILITY`, `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs.rs`), which only the downtime worker of R101.7 names; the colocated analytics pool (`worker_loop`, `executor_loop.rs`) and a remote worker claiming `pool:default` never lease it. No downtime job is submitted with a lane, and the window budget is bounded by the knob ranges of R101.2 and the operator-provisioned capacity cells `downtime:cpu`/`downtime:memory` of R101.3, not by the `SchedulerConfig` lane floors, which count worker slots. (3) The budget stop of a running job is R101.7. A skip writes no job; the selection record of R101.5 is the only write. Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.3`, `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.5` (the skip reason type). | Test: `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::tests::budgetless_type_is_skipped_with_budget_required` (L3): a `dw:JobType` without `dw:hasBudget` yields one `BudgetRequired` skip, no lease request and no submission. Test: `crates/medulla-jobs/src/store.rs::tests::downtime_job_is_never_claimed_by_an_analytics_worker` (L2): with one downtime job queued, `claim_next` with capabilities `[mining.association, pool:default]` returns `None` and with `[downtime]` returns the job. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.5` | **Each deciding tick is recorded as graph facts, and the served read pages them.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Build row. One store: the commons graph holds the selections; the in-memory `LAST_SELECTIONS`, `record_selections` and `latest_selections` of `crates/medulla-server-api/src/downtime.rs` are deleted. (1) Wire types in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/downtime.rs`: `DowntimeSkipReason` (`#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]`, `Copy`): `PreconditionFalse`, `BudgetDoesNotFit`, `LeaseNotGranted`, `BudgetRequired`; `DowntimeSkipped { iri: String, reason: DowntimeSkipReason }`; `DowntimeSelectionRecord { selection_id: String, at_ms: i64, chosen: Vec<DowntimeSelected>, skipped: Vec<DowntimeSkipped> }` (`DowntimeSelected` unchanged); and `DowntimeSelections` becomes `{ selections: Vec<DowntimeSelectionRecord>, next_after: Option<String> }`, all `deny_unknown_fields`. `apply_plan` returns a `DowntimeSelectionRecord`: the plan's precondition and budget skips, the lease skips of `apply_plan` and the `BudgetRequired` skips of R101.4, in that order. (2) Graph facts: when `chosen` or `skipped` is non-empty, `run_tick` commits one batch with `medulla_server_mutation::mutation_batch::commit_internal_graph_methods(InternalGraphCommitRequest::new(Some(&backend), &core, CommitOrigin { request_id: 0, principal: Some(<internal principal fingerprint>) }, "__commons__", &batch_id, methods, &payload))`, with `batch_id = opaque_coordinator_key("downtime-selection", "__commons__", &at_ms.to_string())`. The selection node id is `dw:selection/` plus `i64::MAX - at_ms` as 20 zero-padded digits, so ascending id order is newest first; `Method::AddNode` with properties `{"type": "dw:Selection", "dw:atMs": at_ms, "dw:entryCount": n}`. Entry `i` (chosen first, then skipped, in record order) is node `<selection id>/<i>` with `{"type": "dw:SelectionEntry", "dw:jobType": iri, "dw:priority": p, "dw:cpuMs": c, "dw:memoryBytes": m, "dw:outcome": "chosen"|"skipped", "dw:skipReason": <reason, skipped only>, "dw:jobId": <id, chosen only>, "dw:intent": <intent, chosen only>}` (`DowntimeSelected` carries the intent, so the entry stores it) and an `Method::AddEdge` from the selection node to it with `{"type": "dw:chosen"}` or `{"type": "dw:skipped"}`. Add `dw:Selection`, `dw:SelectionEntry`, `dw:chosen`, `dw:skipped`, `dw:skipReason`, `dw:outcome`, `dw:jobType`, `dw:jobId`, `dw:atMs` and `dw:entryCount` to `crates/medulla-core/ontology/downtime_work-v1.ttl` (MEDULLA-CQ-ORCH-R011.2 reads them with `Sparql`). (3) Prune: the same batch removes every existing selection beyond the newest 999: `run_tick` reads `core.get_nodes_by_label_page("dw:Selection", None, 1_063)` before the commit and adds `Method::RemoveNode` for each selection at index 999 or later and for its `dw:entryCount` entry nodes, so at most 1,000 selections remain. A tick with nothing chosen and nothing skipped writes nothing. A failed commit is logged and the tick still counts its submissions. (4a) Job observation (the read the L4 test of R101.6 uses; engine-tenant jobs are refused by `owned_job` to every caller, so no per-job read exists and none is added): `DowntimeSelected` (`crates/medulla-work-types/src/downtime.rs`) gains `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] pub job: Option<DowntimeJobObservation>`, with `DowntimeJobObservation { state: DowntimeJobState, attempts_made: u32, checkpoint_stage: Option<String>, has_lease: bool, cpu_ms: Option<u64> }` and the closed snake_case enum `DowntimeJobState { Submitted, Running, Publishing, CancelRequested, Backoff, Succeeded, Failed, Cancelled }` (one per `JobState` variant), all `deny_unknown_fields`, `Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize`. It is filled at read time, never stored on the selection node: `pub fn observe_downtime_job(persist_dir: Option<&str>, job_id: &str) -> Option<DowntimeJobObservation>` in `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs.rs` opens the store with `core::job_store(persist_dir)`, reads `JobStore::get(job_id)` and maps `state` (`has_lease` true for `Running`, `Publishing` and `CancelRequested`; `checkpoint_stage` the checkpoint's `stage`; `cpu_ms` from `output.usage` when the job has a result), and answers `None` when the store or job is absent. The handler `downtime_selections` calls it for each chosen entry with the persist directory `ServerState` holds. The scope stays `jobs:read`. (4) Served read: `Method::DowntimeSelections` becomes `DowntimeSelections { #[serde(default)] after: Option<String>, #[serde(default = "default_downtime_page")] limit: u32 }` (default 20, range [1, 100]; outside refuses `INVALID_PARAMS`). The handler in `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/scheduler_config.rs` reads the commons `GraphCore` from `state.registry`, takes `get_nodes_by_label_page("dw:Selection", after, limit + 1)` (newest first), loads each selection's entry nodes by id, and answers the first `limit` with `next_after` set to the last returned id when one more existed. With no commons graph or no selection it answers an empty page. Scope `jobs:read` is unchanged. (5) Budget use: `ResultUsage` (`crates/medulla-jobs/src/result.rs`) gains `#[serde(default)] cpu_ms: u64` and `#[serde(default)] peak_memory_bytes: u64`; the downtime worker of R101.7 fills them, and the existing job result read (`JobStore::get_result`) returns them. Contracts regenerate (`DowntimeSelections` request and reply shapes). Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.3`, `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.7` (usage). | Planned: `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime.rs::tests::selection_recorded` (L3): one tick with three types (one false precondition) writes one `dw:Selection` with two chosen and one `precondition_false` entry; `DowntimeSelections { after: None, limit: 1 }` returns it with `next_after` absent; 1,001 ticks leave exactly 1,000 selections. |
Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.1, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.3
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.6` | **Hot-path proof: idle work runs in priority order and foreground load preempts it.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Write-test row over R101.3 to R101.7. Entry point: a served engine started with `downtime.idle_quiet_secs=10`, `downtime.tick_secs=1`, provisioned capacity cells `downtime:cpu` and `downtime:memory`, and three `dw:JobType` instances of the test intent `test_downtime_steps` (priorities 30, 20, 10; one with a false precondition). The test body is installed only in the test build through `install_downtime_body` (R101.7) and runs 100 ms steps until 20 steps are done. Test build and launch: the test body (`TestStepsBody`, `crates/medulla-server/src/downtime_test_steps.rs`: 100 ms steps, 20 steps) is installed by the `test-support` arm of `seams_install::install_downtime_bodies` (R101.7), so the served binary is built with feature `test-support`, and `server()` of `tests/test_native_no_auth_served.py` gains `extra_args: Sequence[str] = ()` appended to the command line so the test passes `--knob downtime.idle_quiet_secs=10 --knob downtime.tick_secs=1 --knob downtime.idle_max_rps=5 --knob downtime.window_cpu_permille=1000`. The mechanism is entirely R101.7: `ForegroundActivity::note_request` (`crates/medulla-server-api/src/downtime.rs`, called from `dispatch_with_context` in `crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs`) feeds `preempt_requested`, and the downtime worker yields at the next step boundary with `JobStore::yield_running_fenced`; no tick-side cancel or resume runs (`request_cancel_batch` ends a job in terminal `Cancelled`, and `resume_batch` serves retry of a failed job, so neither is used). Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.5`, `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.7`. | Planned: `tests/scheduler/test_downtime_scheduler_served.py::test_idle_window_runs_priority_jobs_and_preempts` (L4): after 10 s idle, `DowntimeSelections` shows the two eligible types chosen in priority order and the third skipped `precondition_false`; each chosen entry's `job.state` is `running` with `job.has_lease` true (the `DowntimeSelected.job` observation of R101.5 (4a), the only served read of an engine-tenant job); then `downtime.idle_max_rps + 1` requests in one second make every chosen entry's `job` read `state == "submitted"`, `has_lease == false`, `has_checkpoint == true` (the `DowntimeJobObservation.has_checkpoint` field, true when the job holds a checkpoint), `checkpoint_stage == "downtime_step"` and an unchanged `attempts_made` within `downtime.preempt_deadline_ms` (250 ms, polled every 25 ms; the job left `Running` by the J13 reclaim of `yield_running_fenced`, since `Running` always holds a lease), and after the burst (polling at most 2 requests a second, below the idle ceiling) the jobs resume from their checkpoint and reach `state == "succeeded"` with `job.cpu_ms` at most their budget. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R101.7` | **A downtime worker runs claimed downtime jobs in bounded steps, stops on budget and yields to foreground load.** Subsystem: durable kernel, scheduler. Build row. (1) Body seam in `crates/medulla-server-api/src/downtime.rs` (under its `jobs` feature): `pub trait DowntimeBody: Send + Sync { fn step(&self, input: DowntimeStepInput<'_>) -> Result<DowntimeStep, String>; }`, `pub struct DowntimeStepInput<'a> { pub job: &'a medulla_jobs::AnalyticsJob, pub state_blob: Option<&'a [u8]>, pub step_deadline_ms: u64 }`, `pub enum DowntimeStep { Continue { state_blob: Vec<u8>, working_bytes: u64 }, Done { result: medulla_jobs::TypedJobResult, working_bytes: u64 } }`, and a process registry `install_downtime_body(intent: &'static str, body: Arc<dyn DowntimeBody>)`, `downtime_body(intent: &str) -> Option<Arc<dyn DowntimeBody>>`, `downtime_body_intents() -> Vec<&'static str>`. Bodies are installed by `crates/medulla-server/src/seams_install.rs::install_seams`; the body of each shipped intent (`plan_evolution`, `live_artifact_refresh`) is owned by its consumer row. `JobsSeam::register_downtime_intents` (`crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs.rs`) registers exactly `downtime_body_intents()` instead of the fixed `DOWNTIME_INTENTS` (deleted), so a type whose intent has no body fails `IntentNotRegistered` and is never submitted. (2) Preempt predicate, pure, in `crates/medulla-jobs/src/downtime.rs`: `pub fn preempt_requested(reading_peak_rps: f64, idle_max_rps: u32) -> bool` answers `reading_peak_rps > f64::from(idle_max_rps)`. (3) Worker: new `crates/medulla-h-jobs/src/jobs/downtime_worker.rs` with `pub(super) async fn downtime_worker_loop(state, store: Arc<JobStore>, timing: AnalyticsWorkerTiming)`; `ensure_job_workers` (R082.9) starts exactly one when `config.analytics_workers > 0` (same clustered rule as the pool). Its `worker_ref` is `opaque_coordinator_key("downtime-worker", "native", "0")` and its only capability `opaque_worker_capability("downtime")`. It calls `store.claim_next` only while `!preempt_requested(FOREGROUND_ACTIVITY.reading(now).peak_rps, knobs().downtime_idle_max_rps)`, else sleeps `timing.poll_ms`. For a claim it looks up `downtime_body(algo.algorithm)` (missing: `fail_attempt_fenced(.., "downtime_body_missing", ..)`), then loops: run one `step` in `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` with `step_deadline_ms = knobs().downtime_preempt_deadline_ms / 2`, add the step's wall-clock milliseconds to `used_cpu_ms` and keep `peak_memory_bytes = max(working_bytes)`; on `Continue` write `checkpoint_fenced(.., Checkpoint { progress, stage: "downtime_step", state_blob: Some(state_blob), .. })` and renew the lease when half of `timing.lease_ms` passed. Budget stop: when `used_cpu_ms > job.policy.resources.cpu_ms` or `peak_memory_bytes > job.policy.resources.memory_bytes`, call `fail_attempt_fenced(.., "downtime_budget_exhausted", ..)`. Preempt: when `preempt_requested` holds at a step boundary, call new `JobStore::yield_running_fenced(job_id, worker_ref, epoch, now_ms) -> Result<AnalyticsJob>` in `crates/medulla-jobs/src/store.rs`: fenced like `checkpoint_fenced`, valid only from `Running` with the caller's live lease, it is the J13 `Reclaim` transition: the job leaves `Running` for `JobState::Submitted { checkpoint: Some(checkpoint) }` (a `Running` state always holds a live `WorkerLease`, so `Running` without a lease does not exist), the attempt `claim_next` counted is given back (`retry.attempts_made -= 1`), and the job is back in the ready index through the same transition write as the other fenced calls, so the next `claim_next` resumes it from `checkpoint.state_blob`. The budget of R101.7 is per claim: `used_cpu_ms` and `peak_memory_bytes` start at zero on every claim (the checkpoint carries only the body's `state_blob`), so a yield resets the meter. Done: set `result.usage = Some(ResultUsage { cpu_ms: used_cpu_ms, peak_memory_bytes, .. })`, then `stage_result_fenced` and `complete_publication_fenced` as `execute_claim` does. A step that returns `Err` fails the attempt as `worker_execution_failed` through the shared `settle_failed_claim`. The failure reasons `downtime_budget_exhausted` and `downtime_body_missing` join the job failure-reason set of R082.8. Shipped intents: `plan_evolution` is a `DowntimeBody` (`PlanEvolutionBody`) installed by `seams_install::install_downtime_bodies`, not an in-process run after the tick; `live_artifact_refresh` has no body (its `LiveArtifactRefresh` work seam is not on main), so its type fails `IntentNotRegistered` and is never submitted until its consumer row installs one. Test seam: under `any(test, feature = "test-support")` `install_downtime_bodies` also installs the body of intent `test_downtime_steps` (100 ms steps, `Done` after 20 steps), so the served test binary built with `test-support` runs it. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.1` | **Served contract.** The five KV methods are `Stable` and carry the Python consumer profile. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_get`, `kv_put`, `kv_delete`, `kv_scan`, `kv_cas` (dispatch_handlers.rs:211-270). Shape: method variants and result types in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/storage_wire/kv.rs and crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/result_contract/storage.rs; stability and the Python profile come from the policy rows in crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs; no new file. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_dispatch_put_get_scan_delete_cas` round-trips every method through the real dispatch. The generated contract lists all five as wire-callable. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.2` | **Scope and authorization.** A private key's store namespace is an opaque digest of tenant, principal, request graph and namespace. All four come from the verified request; no body field widens them. No Redis, S3 or KV-cache adapter namespace can alias a served one. Reads require `kv:read` or the coarse `kg:read`. Writes require `kv:write` or the coarse `kg:write`. The request boundary refuses a missing scope before any row is read. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `KvCall::namespace` (dispatch_handlers.rs:116) → `wire_namespace` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:27); scope names are checked at `is_kv_method` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:680). Shape: the namespace digest is `CarrierAuthority::namespace("kv-namespace", ..)` over graph and local namespace; `resolve_kv_store` (dispatch_handlers.rs) refuses before any row read; scopes `kv:read` and `kv:write` are listed in the policy rows of crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_keys_are_scoped_by_graph_and_writes_need_kv_write`: the same key in another graph is absent. A `kv:read`-only caller reads, and its write is refused with the row unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.3` | **Versioned compare-and-swap and bounded paginated scan.** Every served write stamps the namespace's mutation-kernel commit version. That version never repeats, even after a delete and re-create. `KvCas` swaps only when the stored version equals `expected_version`; `0` means absent. A refused `KvCas` reports the current version. `KvScan` returns at most 1000 rows (default 100) and 4 MiB per page, in key order. Its exclusive-start cursor must lie inside the prefix. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_cas` (dispatch_handlers.rs:270) and `kv_scan` (dispatch_handlers.rs:257) → `versioned_write` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:117) and `scan_redb_page` (wire.rs:236). Shape: the version sequence uses `next_version(stamp, current)` (wire.rs:73) so it never repeats; page bounds are the constants `DEFAULT_WIRE_SCAN_LIMIT`, `MAX_WIRE_SCAN_LIMIT` and `MAX_WIRE_SCAN_PAGE_BYTES` (wire.rs:20-22); `PageBuilder` (wire.rs:272) stops at the byte cap; `scan_start` (wire.rs:218) refuses a cursor outside the prefix. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_dispatch_put_get_scan_delete_cas` covers stale-version refusal, create-if-absent and no A-B-A after delete. `::kv_scan_pages_are_bounded_and_resume_from_the_cursor` reads 25 keys in three pages and refuses a foreign cursor. `crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs::tests::versions_never_repeat_across_delete_and_recreate` covers the row codec. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.4` | **Durable, audited, replayable mutations.** `KvPut`, `KvDelete` and `KvCas` each commit as one native MutationBatch in `kv.redb`. Their durability domain is `KvRedb`, and they commit before they acknowledge. One transaction holds the row, ledger, fence, idempotency record and outbox. They are `audited`: a digest-only `KV_PUT`, `KV_DELETE` or `KV_CAS` line plus the durable batch ledger. They emit no graph CDC, because native outbox rows are the projection authority. `KvDelete` is idempotent; `KvPut` and `KvCas` are not. All three replay by `OperationIdentity`. A fresh nonce with the same operation key returns the stored verdict without a second write. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `KvCall::mutate` (dispatch_handlers.rs:131) → `compile_kv_batch` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:613) → `write_kv_row` (kv.rs:186) → `medulla_kernel::audit::audit_line`. Shape: one `MutationBatch` per call, committed to kv.redb through `swap_in_owner_write` (kv.rs:157); the `audited` flag and `DurabilityDomain::KvRedb` are set in crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs; replay identity comes from `OperationIdentity` in the batch compile of crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile. | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_fresh_nonce_replays_stable_operation_without_duplicate_write`; `crates/medulla-capabilities/tests/consistency` `audited_matches_audit_rs_exactly`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.5` | **Hot-path proof.** The entry point is the served, authenticated dispatch against a durable `kv.redb`. The test sends 400 sequential `KvPut`/`KvGet` pairs of 256-byte values. `KvGet` must finish within 5 ms at p50 and 20 ms at p99. `KvPut` must finish within 25 ms at p50 and 500 ms at p99. Both include envelope verification. `KvPut` also includes the durable commit. On the build host in a debug build, `KvGet` measured 1012 µs at p50 and 1731 µs at p99. There `KvPut` measured 3931 µs at p50 and 7006 µs at p99. On a hosted CI runner, `KvGet` measured 530 µs at p50 and 905 µs at p99. There `KvPut` measured 2151 µs at p50 and 110589 µs at p99. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `kv_put` (dispatch_handlers.rs:218) and `kv_get` (dispatch_handlers.rs:211) after envelope verification in `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102.6` | **Opt-in shared namespace.** Every KV method takes `shared`, which defaults to `false`. A private key stays scoped to the verified tenant, principal, request graph and namespace. `shared: true` drops only the principal. Every principal of the verified tenant and request graph then sees one row. The tenant owns its kernel scope, so writers from different principals advance one version sequence. A shared `KvPut`, `KvDelete` or `KvCas` needs the separate `kv:shared:write` action or `kg:admin`. The coarse `kg:write` and `kv:write` grants are refused. A shared read needs `kv:read`. Caller chain: served `Method::Kv*` → router data plane (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs:260) → `medulla_server_blob::kv::try_handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:586) → `dispatch_handlers::handle` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/dispatch_handlers.rs:178) → `names_shared` (dispatch_handlers.rs:166) sets `KvCall.shared` → `KvCall::scope_owner` (dispatch_handlers.rs:122) and `wire_namespace` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv/wire.rs:27). Shape: `shared` is a field of every `Method::Kv*` variant in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_06.rs and method_07.rs; a shared namespace is `opaque_coordinator_key("kv-shared-namespace", tenant_scope, ..)` and its kernel scope owner is `CarrierAuthority::tenant_scope`; `kv:shared:write` is accepted at `is_kv_method` (crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs:683). | `crates/medulla-server-blob/tests/kv_dispatch.rs::kv_shared_namespace_spans_principals_and_needs_kv_shared_write`: one principal's private key is absent for another. A second principal reads a shared write, which is not the writer's private row. A coarse-write principal is refused. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.750` | **Model.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. An agent turn is a WorkItem with `kind` `agent_turn`. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.751` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. Enqueue counts the tenant in-flight rows with `count_submit_work_item_tenant_inflight` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/submit.rs`. It refuses when the count reaches `max_tenant_in_flight`. Claim and commit stay idempotent by `idempotency_key`. `CancelWorkItem` on a leased item with an unexpired lease returns `InFlight` and stamps `cancel_requested_at_ms` in `cancel.rs`. The next `RenewWorkItemLease` renews the lease and returns `cancel_requested` true. The worker stops at its next step boundary and calls `CommitWorkItemResult` with status `cancelled`. When the lease expires first, `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` in `claim.rs` retires the item as `cancelled` and never returns it to `ready`. The cancel grace is the remaining lease time, so no new knob exists. Caller chain: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem`, `CancelWorkItem`, `RenewWorkItemLease` and `CommitWorkItemResult` → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → `count_submit_work_item_tenant_inflight` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/submit.rs:223), `apply_cancel_work_item_row` (cancel.rs:116), `apply_renew_work_item_lease_row` (lease.rs:13), `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` (claim.rs:111). Shape: extend the existing `cancel_requested_at_ms` property writes in cancel.rs and the renew result in lease.rs (`lease_renewed`, lease.rs:97) with the `cancel_requested` flag; the retire-as-cancelled branch is a new arm next to `retire_exhausted_lease` (claim.rs:48); the result type gains the field in crates/medulla-work-types/src/native_control.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/cancel.rs::tests::in_flight_cancel_stamps_request_and_renew_reports_it` (L1). Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R750.752` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. Enqueue beyond `max_tenant_in_flight` refuses with the typed code `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` and writes nothing. A second turn on a context with a stale head refuses with the conflict of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.3`. A claim capability that cannot be verified refuses as `ACCESS_DENIED` for every verify failure (`unauthorized`, `malformed` and any other denial of the capability verifier); the served answer never names the reason, and the privacy-safe reason (the verify denial class) is written to the audit record only. A cancel of a terminal item returns `Noop` and changes nothing. `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` is registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Shape: `WORK_QUEUE_FULL` is a new variant in `ServerErrorCode` (crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs:8) raised where the in-flight count meets `max_tenant_in_flight` in submit.rs; every capability verify failure maps to the existing `ACCESS_DENIED` at the method-error layer, and the verify denial class goes to the audit record only; cancel of a terminal item is `cancel_status_refusal` (cancel.rs:33). | Test: `tests/durable/test_agent_turn_queue_served.py::test_full_queue_stale_head_and_bad_capability_refuse` (L4) asserts each code and unchanged state, with a cross-tenant case, and that both a forged (`unauthorized`) and a truncated (`malformed`) capability answer `ACCESS_DENIED` with the reason present in the audit record and absent from the answer. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R820.1` | **Model.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. `EffectPhase { Unstarted, Started, Resolved }` is the row key `effect_phase` (`WORK_ITEM_EFFECT_PHASE`) of the WorkItem row and a member of `NATIVE_WORK_ITEM_ROW_KEYS` in `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read.rs`, so generic writers cannot set it (`row_guard.rs`). The same file holds `ReceiptId` (a newtype over `sha256:` plus 64 lowercase hex characters of the SHA-256 of the canonical receipt), `EffectReceipt { job_id, attempt, request_ref, principal_ref, outcome, receipt_ref, committed_at }` with `outcome` the closed set `applied`, `not_applied`, `compensated`, `AdmittedRequest { request_ref, tenant, session_ref, payload_digest, bytes }` (`bytes` at most 1 MiB, a `Fixed` limit; `request_ref` is `sha256:` plus the hex SHA-256 of the canonical request), and `AdmittedRequestRecord`. Receipts live in `work_item_effect_receipts` (`EFFECT_RECEIPTS: TableDefinition<(&str, &str, u64), &[u8]>` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/effect.rs`, keyed `(graph, work_item_id, attempt)`, value the sealed MessagePack of `EffectReceipt`), and admitted requests in `work_item_admitted_requests` (`ADMITTED_REQUESTS`, same key, value the request digest and a payload ref, not the bytes). Both tables are in the GraphShard census (`crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/graph_shard.rs`) behind the predecessor `GRAPH_SHARD_BEFORE_EFFECT_RECEIPTS` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs`) and its `offline_upgrade!` entry; a shard at the old layout refuses to open with `GRAPH_SHARD_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED`, and `store-upgrade` moves it. `WorkItemView` carries `effect_phase` and `effect_receipt: Option<ReceiptId>`, read by `read_work_item` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/read.rs`) from `EFFECT_RECEIPTS` at `(graph, work_item_id, current attempt)`. Caller chain: stored by the WorkItem write arms of `apply_work_item_rows` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs`) and projected by `WorkItemView::from_tenant_row`. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-types/src/work_item_read/tests.rs::effect_records_round_trip_with_golden_digest` (L2) and `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/tests.rs::pre_effect_receipts_shard_upgrades_offline_and_old_layout_refuses_open` (L3). Verified by the root L4 test. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R820.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. Add `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/effect.rs` with `apply_mark_effect_started` and `apply_record_effect_receipt`. `MarkWorkItemEffectStarted` sets `Started` under the live claim before the first effect. Change `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` in `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/claim.rs`. A lease expiry on a `Started` item returns it to `ready` with `attempt` unchanged and never calls `retire_exhausted_lease`. A claim of a `Started` item does not increment `attempt`. The item stays open until a receipt resolves it. `effect.rs` keys every receipt and admitted-request access by `(work_item_id, attempt)`, never by job id; a job id is a field of the receipt and is checked against the item (`EFFECT_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`). `SubmitAdmittedRequest` inserts the digest and payload ref into `work_item_admitted_requests` under `(work_item_id, attempt)` once. `RecordEffectReceipt` inserts the receipt into `work_item_effect_receipts` under `(work_item_id, attempt)`, once, in the same transaction as the terminal commit of `commit.rs`. A replay of an identical receipt returns the stored receipt; a different receipt under the same key refuses with `EFFECT_RECEIPT_CONFLICT`. `GetAdmittedRequest` reads within the caller tenant and session. Every write is a compare-and-set on the item revision, O(1) per call. Shape: new file crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/effect.rs declared in work_item.rs; the `Started` guard is a branch at the top of `claim_reclaim_expired_lease` that calls `reclaim_expired_lease` and skips `retire_exhausted_lease` (claim.rs:48); the receipt write shares the transaction of `commit_transition_result` (lease.rs:151). | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/effect.rs::tests::started_expiry_keeps_attempt_and_blocks_dead_letter` (L1), plus `receipt_replay_returns_stored_receipt`. Verified by the root L4 test. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R820.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, work items. A receipt whose job ID, request reference or principal differs from the work item refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`. A second, different receipt under one `(work_item_id, attempt)` key of `work_item_effect_receipts` refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_CONFLICT`. A receipt whose `attempt` differs from the item's `Started` attempt refuses as `EFFECT_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`. A start mark without a live claim refuses as `WORK_ITEM_CLAIM_STALE`. A request read from another tenant or session returns not found. An admitted request whose bytes do not match its reference refuses as `ADMITTED_REQUEST_DIGEST_MISMATCH`. A request above 1 MiB refuses as `ADMITTED_REQUEST_TOO_LARGE`. All codes are registered in `ServerErrorCode`. A shard at the pre-upgrade GraphShard layout refuses to open with `GRAPH_SHARD_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` naming `store-upgrade`. A refusal changes no durable state. Caller chain: served `RecordEffectReceipt`, `MarkWorkItemEffectStarted`, `SubmitAdmittedRequest`, `GetAdmittedRequest` → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → the arms of effect.rs. Shape: six new variants in crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs; refusals return before any table write, mirroring `unchanged_transition` (lease.rs:138); a foreign tenant or session read returns `None` like `read_work_item` for another tenant. | Test: `tests/durable/test_uncertain_effect_served.py::test_refusals_leave_state_unchanged` (L4) asserts each code, an unchanged item, and a foreign-tenant read that returns not found. |

Each row holds the job ID, item revision, attempt, outcome and timing. It holds no parameters and no credentials. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700` | **Retention cleanup removes aged documents and orphans (rollup).** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. The served method calls the same `cleanup_graph`. A retention policy removes `Document` nodes past their age limit and nodes left without any edge or owner. It works in bounded batches. A batch never removes a node held by a live reference or a legal hold. Children `.1` to `.6` cover model, behavior, wiring, refusal, evidence and hot-path proof. | The root L4 test is `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_held_and_referenced_nodes_survive`. Add `RetentionCleanupRunRequest{graph, dry_run, cursor: Option<String>}`, `RetentionCleanupReport{scanned, removed, held_live_reference, held_legal_hold, next_cursor}` and `RetentionCleanupRecord{graph, cursor_start, cursor_end, scanned, removed, held_live_reference, held_legal_hold, policy_digest, at_ms}` in new `crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs`. The default batch is 1,000. The default age is unset: no policy means no sweep. Caller chain: `RetentionPolicy` is parsed by the tick in `server_startup::retention_cleanup_tick` (crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs, new beside `sealed_expiry_tick` at line 930) and by the `RetentionCleanupRun` handler. The existing `retention_policy.rs` tests cover the invalid shapes. Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. Code: `cleanup_graph(core, policy, cursor, now_ms) -> RetentionCleanupReport` in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs`. Document candidates come from `GraphCore::get_nodes_by_label_page("Document", ..)` in id order after the cursor, and match when `updated_at` is older than `age_ms`. Orphan candidates come from `GraphCore::node_ids()` sorted after the cursor. A node matches when `in_degree` and `out_degree` are 0. It also needs no `owner_ref` metadata key and a `created_at` older than `age_ms`. A step examines at most `batch` candidates and returns `next_cursor` as the last examined id. Each step removes through one `Method::BatchUpdate` of `RemoveNode` operations, so it is one `MutationBatch`. Removal releases the blob holders of the node. Blob garbage collection then applies the grace period of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R050`. The cost is O(n) ids per step plus O(batch) examined nodes. Caller chain: `RetentionCleanupRun` handler arm and the periodic tick (through the `GraphApi` seam, crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/ingest.rs:27) → `cleanup_graph` in crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs → `GraphCore::get_nodes_by_label_page` (used at crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/nodes.rs:192) and `GraphCore::node_ids` (crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_readthrough.rs:63) → one `Method::BatchUpdate`. Shape: the file sits in the graph handler crate so both entry points reach it without one handler crate calling another; the removal batch is built by the same helper that `RemoveNode` writes use in graph_ops/nodes.rs; blob holders are released by the existing node-removal path. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs::tests::step_examines_batch_in_id_order` (L1) runs fixtures of 10, 1,000 and 100,000 nodes and asserts the batch bound and cursor order. Verified by the root L4 test. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. Config: `MEDULLA_RETENTION_CLEANUP` holds the `RetentionPolicy` as JSON, default unset and disabled. `MEDULLA_RETENTION_CLEANUP_SECS` is the sweep interval, default 3600, [60, 86400], seconds. Caller: `spawn_periodic_sweep` in `src/main.rs`, armed beside the sealed-record sweep → `server_startup::retention_cleanup_tick`. Add `RetentionCleanupRun` to `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs` with scope `retention:run` and the handler arm in `crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/`. Both entry points attribute removals to the actor `engine:retention-cleanup` and run on the maintenance lane. Arbora serves the method as `medulla.storage.RetentionCleanupRun` with no new arbora row. The served method commits through the mutation gateway and attributes its removals to `engine:retention-cleanup`; the periodic tick re-enters the same served method under the service actor of that name, one step per resident graph, so the sweep and the method run one code path. The wire cursor is the JSON of the document and orphan cursors, and a step that scans nothing restarts the graph's scan on the next sweep. With no valid policy the served method refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and the sweep is not armed. Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.2`. Caller chain: `spawn_periodic_sweep` (src/main.rs:800) armed beside the sealed-record sweep (src/main.rs:1843) → `server_startup::retention_cleanup_tick` (crates/medulla-server/src/server_startup.rs) → `GraphApi` seam → `cleanup_graph` ; served `Method::RetentionCleanupRun` → router → handler arm in crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops. Shape: config parsing copies `configured_policy` and `sweep_interval_secs` of crates/medulla-server/src/sealed_retention.rs:74,83; the method policy row (scope `retention:run`) goes in crates/medulla-capabilities/src/domains/storage.rs and the variant in crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method. | Test: `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_run_method_matches_sweep` (L4) calls the served method with `dry_run` true and false. It asserts the same report as the sweep. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.4` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. The engine never removes a node with a live inbound edge from outside the batch. It never removes a node whose metadata sets `legal_hold` to true. The hold checks are `Fixed` and cannot be disabled. A policy without a hold check refuses at registration as `RETENTION_HOLD_CHECK_REQUIRED`. A policy with zero age or an out-of-range batch refuses as `RETENTION_POLICY_INVALID`. A request without the scope `retention:run` refuses as `ACCESS_DENIED` (the generic method gate enforces the scope from the policy row of `Method::RetentionCleanupRun`; this row adds no second check). A malformed `MEDULLA_RETENTION_CLEANUP` value (not JSON) refuses as `RETENTION_POLICY_INVALID`; an unset value leaves the served request `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (no policy configured). A refusal leaves durable state unchanged. Both retention codes are registered in `ServerErrorCode`. Caller chain: `RetentionCleanupRun` handler arm → `cleanup_graph` (crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs) hold checks → refusal through `ServerErrorCode`. Shape: `in_degree` and `out_degree` checks and the `legal_hold` metadata test run per candidate before the batch is built; policy registration refuses in `RetentionPolicy::validate` (crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/retention_policy.rs); two new codes in crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs; the `retention:run` scope is checked at the request boundary. | Test: `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_refusals_leave_state_unchanged` (L4) asserts `RETENTION_HOLD_CHECK_REQUIRED`, `RETENTION_POLICY_INVALID`, a request naming another graph, and an unchanged graph. It runs on the no-auth native server, where the anonymous caller holds every scope, so it cannot reach `ACCESS_DENIED`; that code is proven by the Rust served test `tests/retention_cleanup_scope_served.rs::retention_run_without_the_scope_is_access_denied` (L3), which sends `Method::RetentionCleanupRun` through `signed_request_with_scopes` (`tests/common/mod.rs:378`) with a scope set lacking `retention:run` and asserts `ACCESS_DENIED` and an unchanged graph. |
Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.2, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.3
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R700.5` | **Evidence.** Subsystem: durable kernel, maintenance lane. Each non-dry step writes one node `RetentionCleanupRecord` in the same `MutationBatch` as the removals. The batch keeps the newest 100 records per graph. The record holds counts, both cursors and the policy digest. The counter `medulla_retention_cleanup_removed_total{reason}` uses reasons `aged_document` and `orphan`. A dry run writes nothing and returns the report. A read of the record reproduces the report that the step returned. Caller chain: `cleanup_graph` (crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/retention_cleanup.rs) builds the record node in the same `Method::BatchUpdate` as the removals. Shape: `RetentionCleanupRecord` from crates/medulla-mutation-types/src/retention_cleanup.rs; pruning keeps the newest 100 per graph in the same batch; counter `medulla_retention_cleanup_removed_total{reason}` registered with the other kernel series in `crates/medulla-kernel/src/metrics/series.rs` (field `retention_cleanup_removed_total`, recorder `retention_cleanup_removed` in `metrics/retention.rs`) and incremented by the served handler after its batch commits (the tick re-enters the same handler, so there is one increment site); the orphan scan skips nodes labelled `RetentionCleanupRecord` (a record has no edges and would otherwise age out as an orphan). | Test: `tests/durable/test_retention_cleanup_served.py::test_record_reproduces_report` (L4) reads the stored record after a step and compares it to the returned report. |

Entry points: the answer cache of `crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs` and the shared block store of `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs`. It uses reuse rate and rebuild cost and evicts by value density. It expires answers by the volatility class of their dependencies. Children `.1` to `.6` cover model, behavior, wiring, refusal, evidence and hot-path proof. Consumers: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018` and `MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R018.2`. | The root L4 test is `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_answer_entry_invalidated_when_dependency_advances`. It binds every child. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.1` | **Model.** `CacheEntryMeta{key_digest, tier, bytes, hits, rebuild_cost_ms, deps:[GenerationRef]}` and `TierPolicy{max_bytes, min_worth}` in `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs`. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:137) is called by the answer cache `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110) and by `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206). Shape: `CacheEntryMeta`, `GenerationRef` and `TierPolicy` already exist in crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs (lines 19, 26, 47) with `CacheEntryMeta::worth` (line 37); tests extend crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::low_worth_entry_is_not_admitted`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.2` | **Behavior.** `worth = (hits + 1) * rebuild_cost_ms / bytes`, Laplace-smoothed so a first admit (zero hits) has a positive worth and is not refused for having no history; a zero-byte entry has worth 0. Admission needs worth at or above `min_worth`. Eviction removes the lowest worth first from a heap in O(log N). An answer entry is invalid once any dependency generation advances. It is also invalid once its volatility window passes. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (policy.rs:137), `PolicyTier::lookup` (policy.rs:179) and `PolicyTier::evict_lowest` (policy.rs:206), reached from `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110). Shape: `CacheEntryMeta::worth` (policy.rs:37) is the one formula and changes from `hits as f64 * ...` to `(hits + 1) as f64 * ...`; the eviction heap is a `BinaryHeap` keyed on worth inside `PolicyTier`; `set_generation` (policy.rs:114) advances a dependency; tests are `lowest_worth_is_evicted_first` and `dependency_advance_and_volatility_window_end_the_hit` in crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::lowest_worth_is_evicted_first` and planned `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::first_admit_has_positive_worth`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.3` | **Wiring.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. `PolicyTier` has no production caller today. `ResultCache` holds it beside the `DepSet` check: `DepSet` with `DepClock` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/dep_scope) decides whether a cached answer is still valid, and `PolicyTier` decides whether the answer is admitted; a hit needs both. Caller 1: `cache_dep_result` in `crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs` admits each answer through `PolicyTier::admit`. The call passes the tenant and the dependency generations of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R017` and `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R018`. It sets `rebuild_cost_ms` to the measured execution time. `ResultCache::get` reads through `PolicyTier::lookup`. Caller 2: `SharedKvStoreBackend::put` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs` admits each prompt-prefix block. A write that advances a dependency calls `PolicyTier::set_generation`. Knobs, read from `KnobConfig` of `MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R058` and never from the environment by this crate: `knob kvcache.min_worth` default 0.001, [0, 1000], milliseconds saved per byte per hit. `knob kvcache.answer_max_bytes` default 256 MiB, [1 MiB, 64 GiB], bytes. The cache is a pure optimization: a refusal is a miss. Depends: `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.2`, `MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R058`. Caller chain: served query dispatch → `cache_dep_result` (crates/medulla-query-cache/src/result_cache.rs:110) → `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:137); HTTP `PUT /kv/<hash>` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs:201) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (shared_store.rs:206) → `PolicyTier::admit`. Shape: add `medulla-kvcache` as a dependency of crates/medulla-query-cache and hold a `PolicyTier` inside `ResultCache` (result_cache.rs); `GraphCore` receives `kvcache.min_worth` and `kvcache.answer_max_bytes` from `KnobConfig` at construction (a `TierPolicy` argument of `GraphCore::new` in crates/medulla-graph-core/src/graph/core_state.rs, passed by `medulla-server` startup after `load_knobs`) and builds `ResultCache::with_policy(TierPolicy)`; the shared block store receives the same `TierPolicy` from startup; neither crate reads an environment variable; `rebuild_cost_ms` is the elapsed time the dispatch already measures around execution. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_answer_entry_invalidated_when_dependency_advances` (L4) serves one cached query, advances a dependency, and asserts the next call recomputes. `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::dependency_advance_invalidates_answer` (L1) covers the policy. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.4` | **Refusal.** Three inputs return a miss with no content. The first is an entry larger than the tier. The second is a dependency list that names an unknown generation. The third is a read for another tenant's key. The engine never returns a stale hit. Caller chain: `PolicyTier::admit` (policy.rs:137) and `PolicyTier::lookup` (policy.rs:179) with a tenant argument, reached from `cache_dep_result` and `ResultCache::get`. Shape: refusals return `None` or `false`, never an error; the unknown-generation check reads the map `set_generation` fills (policy.rs:114); tenant isolation keys every entry by `(tenant, key_digest)` as `contains` (policy.rs:130) already does. | Planned: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::oversize_entry_is_refused`. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.5` | **Evidence.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. Add `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/metrics.rs` with the counter `medulla_kvcache_policy_total{tier,event}`. The events are `admitted`, `refused`, `evicted`, `invalidated`, `hit` and `miss`. The route `GET /kv/stats` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs` also returns the hit ratio and a replayable trace of the newest 1,024 decisions. Each trace entry holds the key digest, the event, the worth (the Laplace-smoothed value of `.2`) and the refusal reason. It never holds a key or a value. The trace holds no tenant data beyond a tenant digest. Caller chain: `GET /kv/stats` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs, stats probe near line 362) → counters updated by `PolicyTier` callers in `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (shared_store.rs:206). Shape: the counter lives in the wire-msg crate that owns the HTTP surface, not in a server module; the trace is a fixed-size ring of 1,024 entries kept O(1) so the stats probe stays O(1); entries hold `key_digest`, event, worth and reason only. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_stats_report_policy_counters_and_trace` (L4) drives admissions and evictions and compares the counters and trace. `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_policy.rs::foreign_tenant_key_misses` (L1) covers tenant isolation. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R800.6` | **Hot-path proof.** Subsystem: durable kernel, cache tier. Entry point: the served query path of `.3` and the nightly benchmark `crates/medulla-kvcache/benches/tier_policy.rs`. The benchmark runs 1 million admit and lookup operations. Budget: lookup p50 under 5 microseconds, lookup p99 under 50 microseconds, admission overhead under 10 percent of an unpoliced insert. The benchmark never gates a pull request. The served test proves the behavior, not the speed. Caller chain: served query path of R800.3 → `PolicyTier::lookup` and `PolicyTier::admit` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/policy.rs:179,137). Shape: new bench crates/medulla-kvcache/benches/tier_policy.rs (add a `[[bench]]` entry in crates/medulla-kvcache/Cargo.toml) running one million operations, building the tier from the `KnobConfig` defaults of `.3`; the served test proves behaviour only. The cold answer of the served test has a rebuild cost low enough that its smoothed worth stays under `kvcache.min_worth`. | Test: `tests/test_kvcache_policy_served.py::test_policy_admits_hot_answer_and_refuses_cold_one` (L4). The nightly receipt (L6) meets the budget. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4` | **Run ownership binds at native admission.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem` with a run admission request → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs::apply_work_item_rows` → `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs::admit_owner`. A single-run admission records the verified caller as the owner inside the WorkItem transaction. The WorkItem ID is `work_item_id(graph, tenant, run_id)`. A caller-provided ID, a batch run admission and a generic write that changes an owner binding all refuse with `ACCESS_DENIED`. A duplicate admission refuses with `CONFLICT`. Replay authority stays mandatory. Children `.2` to `.7` keep recovery, replay and performance. Consumer: ARBORA-A2A-R001.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/store_run_owner.rs::admission_binds_owner_and_refuses_duplicate_forged_and_generic_writes` (L2). `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_through_signed_dispatch` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.1` | **Recover immutable run submission records.** `GetRunWorkItem` reads the WorkItem and original submission from one durable snapshot. Graph, tenant, run and verified owner select the record. Missing and foreign records return null. Missing or malformed historical evidence returns non-retryable `RUN_ADMISSION_UNAVAILABLE`. Completion, lease expiry and restart preserve the original submission. Generic writes cannot plant, replace or remove it. Reading grants no execution authority. Caller chain: served `Method::GetRunWorkItem` → `route_owner_bound_work_item_reads` (crates/medulla-server-graph/src/dispatch/graph_pipeline/native_routes.rs:305) → `read_admission` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs:70). Shape: one snapshot read in run_owner.rs using `snapshot_row` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/read.rs:36); the immutable binding is guarded by `row_guard.rs` so generic writers cannot plant, replace or remove it; types in crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_history.rs. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/store_run_owner.rs::run_admission_recovery_preserves_original_request` (L2) and `::run_admission_recovery_refuses_missing_evidence` (L2) cover restart, owner isolation and invalid evidence; `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_owner_read_survives_native_changes_and_restart` (L3) covers authenticated dispatch. Point lookup costs O(log N + B) time and O(B) memory for B bounded submission bytes. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.2` | **Owner-bound recovery meets a 10 ms p99.** Subsystem: work items. Wire row. Caller chain: nightly workflow → `benches/redb_write_latency_bench.rs` `main` → `work_items::run_owner_recovery` (`benches/redb_write_latency_bench/work_items.rs`) → `run_owner::read_admission` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/run_owner.rs`). The group seeds 10 000 WorkItems through `test_shard::submit_run_rows` and times `read_admission` at a fixed stride; `P99_BUDGET_MS` is 10.0, and the receipt (`claim::write_receipt`, `BenchmarkClaim` fields: workload, build profile, hardware, p50, p99) is written under `benches/receipts/`. The `test-support` visibility of `test_shard` and `run_fixture` (`seed_claim`, `admission`, `with_rows` public) and the root `[dev-dependencies]` line `medulla-work-store = { path = "crates/medulla-work-store", features = ["test-support"] }` are in place. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.5` | **Tier ladder from accelerator memory to durable storage.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT /kv/<hash>` → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` → `TieredCache::put` in `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs`. `SharedKvStoreBackend` holds a `TieredCache<String, Block>` whose cold tier is the block namespace `kvcache-blocks` inside `kv.redb`, the one durable copy of a block (no `eg_kvcache_cold` table and no second file; `RedbColdStore`, its `durable` feature and its table are removed in the same change, and the offline upgrade `KV_BEFORE_NAMESPACE_COLD` in `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/lineage.rs` deletes the table from an existing `kv.redb` while keeping every `kv` row, so a normal open of the old layout is refused with `KV_FORMAT_UPGRADE_REQUIRED` and the pinned `Kv` layout digest changes with it). The cold tier is a new `KvNamespaceColdStore` implementing the `ColdStore<String>` trait of `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/cold.rs` over the engine's `KvStore`, so every cold write is an admitted transaction of the mutation kernel. The HOT, WARM and COLD byte budgets are typed fields of `KnobConfig` (`MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R058`): `kvcache_hot_bytes: u64` (knob `kvcache.hot_bytes`, env `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_HOT_BYTES`) defaults to 1 GiB, `kvcache_warm_bytes: u64` (`kvcache.warm_bytes`, `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_WARM_BYTES`) defaults to 4 GiB, and `kvcache_cold_bytes: u64` (`kvcache.cold_bytes`, `MEDULLA_KVCACHE_COLD_BYTES`) defaults to 64 GiB. Each range is [64 MiB, 1 TiB] inclusive, unit bytes; the startup bounds check refuses a value outside it with `KNOB_OUT_OF_RANGE`. Byte pressure demotes the lowest-scored block one rung: hot, compressed warm, durable cold. A hit promotes the block to hot. A demotion moves the bytes and never drops them. A put writes the block through to its cold row before it acknowledges, a get that misses the ladder adopts the cold row, and `GET /kv/stats` reports the per-tier counts under `tiers`. The connector offloads from accelerator memory through `medulla/kvcache/connector.py`. Caller chain: HTTP `PUT /kv/<hash>` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/mod.rs:201, `KvCacheStore::put`) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206) → `TieredCache::put` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:319). Shape: budgets are read from `KnobConfig` where `SharedKvStoreBackend` is built; the cold rung is `KvNamespaceColdStore` (new, `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/cold_namespace.rs`) over the `kvcache-blocks` namespace of `kv.redb`; compression is crates/medulla-kvcache/src/compress.rs; the new test file is crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs and the served test goes under tests/kvcache_http_served.rs. Depends: MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R058. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs::demote_then_promote_keeps_bytes` (L2) fills each tier past budget and reads every block back byte-identical. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::over_budget_put_demotes_and_get_promotes` (L3). `crates/medulla-storage/src/owner/agent_library_upgrade/tests.rs::kv_upgrade_keeps_every_kv_row_deletes_the_cold_table_and_runs_once` (L2) upgrades a file written in the old layout. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.6` | **Graph-guided paging policy.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT /kv/version/<n>` (the graph write hook) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::refresh_importance` → `TieredCache::apply_importance_scores` (`tiered.rs`). On a version bump the body carries the graph edges (`{"edges":[[a,b],..]}`, a bad body is refused with HTTP 400) and the backend's `refresh_importance(edges)` clears the derived importance and applies the scores of `graph_importance::pagerank_importance`; derived importance lives only in the ladder and is never stored as a row. The demotion score is `recency + knob kvcache.importance_weight * importance`, default 1.0, range [0, 10], unit ratio (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179). A GET names the request's graph neighbors in the header `x-medulla-neighbors`, and a hit prefetches those cold blocks, read from the `kvcache-blocks` namespace of `kv.redb` (R083.5), whose graph neighbors the request reads (`get_with_graph_prefetch`). Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.5, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R179.1. Caller chain: `PUT /kv/version/<n>` (kvcache_http/mod.rs, version-bump route) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::refresh_importance` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:337) → `TieredCache::apply_importance_scores` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:289); a hit uses `get_with_graph_prefetch` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/tiered.rs:440). Shape: scores come from `graph_importance::pagerank_importance` (crates/medulla-kvcache/src/graph_importance.rs:35, opt-in feature `graph`); the demotion score adds `knob kvcache.importance_weight` times importance to the recency term inside `TieredCache`; test file crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/graph_paging.rs. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/graph_paging.rs::central_blocks_stay_hot_and_neighbors_prefetch` (L2) exists. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::version_bump_refreshes_importance_and_central_block_outlives_peripheral` (L3). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.7` | **Long-context coherence across tiers.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `GET /kv/<hash>` → `TieredCache::get`. A context of 10 000 000 tokens, in blocks of 256 tokens, stays coherent across all tiers. Coherent means each paged-in block digest equals its digest at first write. The tier never serves a block under another model version or token-prefix hash. Cold blocks are read from the `kvcache-blocks` namespace of `kv.redb` (R083.5). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R083.8` | **Tier refusals.** Subsystem: KV cache. Caller: `PUT` and `GET /kv/<hash>` → `TieredCache`. A failed durable cold write (a failed `kv.redb` transaction on the `kvcache-blocks` namespace, or a write that would exceed `kvcache_cold_bytes`) keeps the block in the warm tier and returns `KV_COLD_WRITE_FAILED` as HTTP 507. A page-in whose digest differs from the stored digest drops the block and returns `KV_BLOCK_DIGEST_MISMATCH` as HTTP 422. Both codes are registered in the KV-cache error enum of `crates/medulla-kvcache/src/contract.rs`. Caller chain: `PUT` and `GET /kv/<hash>` (kvcache_http/mod.rs) → `SharedKvStoreBackend::store_block` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/shared_store.rs:206) and `fetch` → `TieredCache::put` and `get` → `KvNamespaceColdStore` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/kvcache_http/cold_namespace.rs). Shape: the two codes are variants of the error enum in crates/medulla-kvcache/src/contract.rs; the HTTP layer maps them to 507 and 422 in kvcache_http/mod.rs; a failed cold write leaves the block in the warm map of `TieredCache`. | Tests: `crates/medulla-kvcache/tests/tier_ladder.rs::cold_write_failure_and_digest_mismatch_refuse` (L2) asserts each code and an unchanged tier state. `tests/kvcache_http_served.rs::refusal_codes_map_to_507_and_422` (L3). |
