| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.3` | **RESP listener.** Subsystem: durable kernel, wire listeners. Caller: `spawn_redis_listener` in `src/main.rs` starts `redis_wire::serve` of `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire` when the build has feature `redis-wire` and `MEDULLA_REDIS_ADDR` is set. A bare enable token binds `127.0.0.1:6379`. `AUTH <principal> <credential>` resolves the principal through the credential store into a verified request context, the same context a signed request carries; a failed `AUTH` answers `-NOAUTH` and binds nothing. Every served command then enters the dispatch with that context and maps to the served KV methods of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102`: `GET` to `KvGet`, `SET` to `KvPut`, `DEL` to `KvDelete`, `EXISTS` and `MGET` to one `KvGet` per key, and `INCR` to a `KvGet` followed by `KvCas` on the read revision (an integer value; a lost race retries up to the knob `resp.incr_retry_limit`, default 8, then answers `-ERR INCR_CONFLICT`; a non-integer value answers `-ERR value is not an integer`). Only these string commands are served. Hash, list, set, sorted-set and TTL commands (`HSET`, `LPUSH`, `SADD`, `ZADD`, `EXPIRE`, `TTL` and their families), the transaction verbs `MULTI`, `EXEC` and `DISCARD` (the served KV methods commit one key at a time, so no atomic multi-command transaction exists), and `SET` with options (`EX`, `PX`, `NX`, `XX`) are refused with `-ERR RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED` and change nothing. Each verified principal receives an isolated keyspace: the namespace is the principal's private KV scope, so two principals never see each other's keys. Add the integration test `tests/resp_roundtrip.rs`. The in-module tests `eg174_listener_roundtrip_over_tcp` and `unsupported_families_are_refused_before_the_engine` in `crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire/mod.rs` are the unit proof. The listener owns no store and no `kv.redb` of its own; publish/subscribe stays in-process and never touches the KV. Caller chain: `spawn_redis_listener` (src/main.rs:1276) → `medulla_wire_msg::redis_wire::serve` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/redis_wire/mod.rs:2539) → `dispatch` (redis_wire/mod.rs:2048) → `execute_data` (mod.rs:1943). Shape: today `execute_data` calls the KV store of crates/medulla-server-blob/src/kv.rs directly through the per-principal namespace of `pseudonymous_broker_actor` (mod.rs:1567). This row changes `AUTH` to build the verified request context from the credential store and the string commands to enter the served KV methods of `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R102` through the dispatch, so the listener shares the admission and policy path of every other wire. The unsupported families are removed from `execute_data` and answer `RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED`. The namespace is the principal's private KV scope. The row adds tests/resp_roundtrip.rs. | Test: `tests/resp_roundtrip.rs::set_get_maps_to_kv_methods` (L3) writes with `SET` and reads the value back through `KvGet` under the same principal, covers `DEL`, `EXISTS`, `MGET` and `INCR`, asserts `RESP_COMMAND_UNSUPPORTED` for `HSET`, `LPUSH`, `SADD`, `ZADD` and `EXPIRE` with no state change, and that a second principal does not see the first principal's keys. |
Depends: none
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R860.4` | **S3-compatible object API.** Subsystem: durable kernel, wire listeners. The build needs feature `s3-api` and the variable `MEDULLA_S3_ADDR` must be set. A bare enable token binds `127.0.0.1:9000`. Startup fails closed unless the access and secret credentials are injected at runtime. Put, get, list and delete map to content-addressed blobs and the durable KV listing index. An object is stored as a blob manifest owned by the listener's fixed service principal (`service:s3-api-client`), and its ETag is the blob digest, so the served `BlobFetchBegin` and `BlobChunkGet` of that principal read the object by that digest. Every request needs `SigV4` authentication. Add the integration test `tests/s3_roundtrip.rs`. Caller chain: `spawn_s3_listener` (src/main.rs:1350) → `medulla_wire_msg::s3::serve` (crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/s3/mod.rs:1137) → `serve_with_blob_store` (s3/mod.rs:1122) → routes in s3/route.rs. Shape: SigV4 verification is in crates/medulla-wire-msg/src/s3/mod.rs with `calendar.rs`; unsigned requests end at the `AccessDenied` response (mod.rs:909); add tests/s3_roundtrip.rs with a signed client. The client is the dev-dependency `aws-sigv4` (Apache-2.0, minimum version `1`) in `[dev-dependencies]` of the root `Cargo.toml`: it signs each request with `Authorization` header signing, and the test sends the signed request over the `reqwest` already in the tree. The server verifies only the `Authorization` header; presigned query authentication is out of scope and a presigned request is refused with `AccessDenied`. The full AWS SDK is not added. | Test: `tests/s3_roundtrip.rs::put_get_list_delete` (L3) drives each operation through a standard S3-compatible client with signed requests. |

It commits the terminal row, receipt and one digest-bound outbox event together. Counters contain only observed nonnegative exact integers; omitted usage stays unknown, not zero. Failed or cancelled runs cannot publish an answer reference. Owner-bound recovery reads the exact receipt from one snapshot. Terminal replay preserves the first receipt. Ordinary completion and delegated outcome extensions are exclusive. Shape: `RunCompletionInput` and the receipt types are in crates/medulla-work-types/src/run_completion.rs; usage counters in run_usage.rs; the terminal row, receipt and one digest-bound outbox event are written in one transaction by commit.rs using the engine commit clock `committed_at_ms`. | Test: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/store_run_owner.rs::ordinary_completion_native_ledger_outbox_restart_atomicity` (L2), `::ordinary_completion_native_fence_and_terminal_noop_preserve_binding` (L2) and `::ordinary_completion_requires_original_run_owner` (L2). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R861.1` | **Lifecycle replay compares the stored identity.** The engine answers a `CreateGraph` or `DeleteGraph` retry from the stored lifecycle receipt. It compares the receipt's retained identity: graph, graph type, principal, tenant scope and idempotency key. It never recompiles the batch to compare digests. So a receipt that the previous patch release wrote replays instead of conflicting. A receipt with a different identity stays an idempotency conflict. The engine refuses an attempt that re-presents the nonce its receipt consumed as REPLAY_NONCE_CONSUMED, before any identity check. Only a fresh attempt under the same idempotency key replays. Shape: the comparison reads the retained identity fields (graph, graph type, principal, tenant scope, idempotency key) of the stored lifecycle receipt and never calls the batch compiler of crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile; the consumed-nonce check runs first in the replay ledger of crates/medulla-table-store. | A unit test shows that a stored receipt with the same identity and different bytes counts as committed, that a different graph type, principal or key is a conflict, and that the engine refuses the consumed nonce and replays a fresh one: `crates/medulla-server-store-tests/src/split_tests/mutation_batch_lifecycle.rs::lifecycle_replay_compares_identity_not_recompiled_bytes` (L2). | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R862.1` | **Restore stages and adopts each bundle shard, then moves graphs out of the staged copy.** Stage: durable kernel, recovery. Caller: `medulla-server restore` → `src/offline_tools/restore.rs::run` → `backup::restore_bundle` (`crates/medulla-persistence/src/backup.rs:860`) → `restore_graph_shards`. The admin cluster handler (`crates/medulla-h-admin/src/admin/cluster.rs:492`) reaches the same path. Decision: restore adds no copy graft and no graft mode. The graft is a move. Its phase A writes a fence marker into the source. Its phase C retires the source binding and owner payload (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/graft.rs`). A read-only bundle can carry neither write. A copy mode would skip the marker, the fence and the retirement, so it would be a second, unfenced route. Main already has one relocation entry point at the restore boundary. It is `inspect_staged_mutation_store` then `adopt_staged_mutation_store` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/adopt.rs:177` and `:286`). The admin store and every bundled store restore through it today. Graph shards must use it too. Rule: `restore_graph_shards` creates the directory `<persist_dir>/.restore-staging`. For each graph shard file the manifest lists, restore copies the bundle file into staging with `std::fs::copy`. It hashes the staged copy and compares the digest with `manifest.file_digests`. It then calls `inspect_staged_mutation_store(path, PhysicalStoreIdentity::new(SHARD_PHYSICAL_STORE)?, OwnerLayout::GraphShard, None)`. `SHARD_PHYSICAL_STORE` is the constant in `crates/medulla-store/src/redb_store/shard.rs`. It passes the token to `adopt_staged_mutation_store`. Restore then calls `shard_migrate::migrate_shards(staging, persist_dir, k)`, unchanged. The move graft retires each scope in the staged copy only. Restore removes the staging directory before `shard_census`, on success and on every error. The bundle directory is never opened writable. No bundle byte changes. Code: one new private function `stage_bundle_shards(bundle_dir: &Path, staging: &Path, manifest: &BackupManifest) -> Result<(), String>` in `backup.rs`. `validate_manifest_file_digests` (`backup.rs:690`) prefixes its error with `ARCHIVE_DIGEST_MISMATCH: ` and names the file. No new type, trait or graft function is added. `graft_graph_from`, `graft_scope_with_payload`, `migrate_shards` and `Shard::open` stay unchanged. Refusals: `ARCHIVE_DIGEST_MISMATCH` fires when a bundle file or its staged copy differs from its manifest digest. It is registered as `ServerErrorCode::ArchiveDigestMismatch` (`crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs:24`). A staging directory that already exists refuses with `restore target already contains a restore staging directory`. That message follows the existing restore-target refusals of `restore_admin_store`. A staged file that is not a graph shard refuses with the existing adoption error `staged mutation owner authority does not match provider`. A staged file that changes before adoption refuses with `staged mutation image changed before atomic adoption`. Every refusal leaves no graph shard file in `persist_dir` and no staging directory. Writes: staging copies, then destination stores only. Budget: staging adds one sequential copy of the bundle shard bytes. Peak extra disk equals the bundle shard total. Restore time stays under twice the copy time of those bytes. Benchmark: the timed restore leg of `tests/test_store_copy_restore.py` (R862.3). Depends: none. | Test: `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/shard_migrate_tests/recovery.rs::copied_bundle_restores_by_content` (L2). It copies a bundle to new inodes and restores the copy. It reads every graph back. It asserts each bundle file digest is unchanged and the staging directory is gone. Test: `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/shard_migrate_tests/recovery.rs::one_changed_byte_refuses_and_leaves_destination_empty` (L2). It flips one byte in one bundle shard and asserts `ARCHIVE_DIGEST_MISMATCH` naming that file, with no shard and no staging left. Test: `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/shard_migrate_tests/recovery.rs::existing_staging_directory_refuses` (L2). Pre-existing proof that must stay green: the `restore_bundle` cases of `crates/medulla-persistence-tests/src/backup_tests.rs`. Verified by the root slice test `tests/test_store_copy_restore.py::test_copied_bundle_restores_and_serves` (planned). |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R862.2` | **Wiring: an explicit command re-binds a copied data directory.** Stage: durable kernel, operator commands. Caller: `medulla-server store-upgrade rebind <data-dir> --copied-from <source-data-dir> --confirm` → `OperatorCommand::StoreUpgrade` with new verb `StoreUpgradeVerb::Rebind` in `src/operator_command.rs` → `store_upgrade::rebind` in new `crates/medulla-persistence-ops/src/store_upgrade/rebind.rs` (planned). Code: the rebind step is the existing `medulla_storage::rebind_copied_store(path, copied_from)` (`crates/medulla-storage/src/recovery/authority.rs:151`). No new rebind function is added. It refuses unless the copy carries the root of `copied_from`. It writes the root derived from the copy's own path and rebinds the serving scopes in one transaction. The command passes each copied store with the same-named store under `--copied-from`. Add `store_root_is_current(path) -> Result<bool, String>` beside it. It reads `STORE_ROOT` through a read-only open and compares it with the root derived from the file's own path. Rule: `rebind` holds the engine lock of `crates/medulla-kernel/src/persist_lock.rs`. For each store from `discover(data_dir)` it runs `validate_recovery_store_read_only` on the source store, read only, and records `recovery_store_fingerprint` of that source. The read-only check applies to the source, not to the copy: a copied store fails it by design, so the check is never run on the copy before the rebind. `recovery_store_fingerprint` covers the data tables only (the ledger tables of `visit_ledger_tables!`) and excludes the scope-binding rows (`STORE_ROOT` and the per-scope bindings), because the rebind rewrites those. The fingerprint is computed on the source and on the copy before the rebind; they must be equal, else `REBIND_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED` names the store and nothing is written. The rebind then runs, and the copy's fingerprint is computed again and must equal the source's; a difference is the same error and names the store. Without `--confirm` it writes nothing and exits 1. A store for which `store_root_is_current` is true is skipped. When every store is skipped, the run reports `nothing to do` and exits 0. A copied store with no same-named store under `--copied-from` refuses with `REBIND_SOURCE_MISSING`, and nothing is written. The ordinary open still refuses a copied store. Exit codes follow the existing `store-upgrade` table. Refusals: `REBIND_NOT_CONFIRMED`, `REBIND_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED`, `REBIND_SOURCE_MISSING`, plus the two existing `rebind_copied_store` refusals. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.3` | **Reserve ordered conversation turns with native run admission.** Optional version-one `ConversationTurnAdmission` binds a context ID, expected head revision and exact preceding run. The existing `SubmitWorkItem` transaction stores a protected head in the graph-incarnation-scoped NODES table with the new WorkItem and immutable original request. Revision zero creates a head. A later reservation checks the same tenant, verified owner, context and preceding run in that write snapshot. Only a successful preceding run may advance the head. Active, failed, cancelled, dead-letter, missing or unknown predecessors refuse. Stale revisions conflict; no branch or implicit queue is created. Generic writers cannot create, replace or remove a head. Existing contexts with a head refuse metadata-only admission. Ordinary requests omit the new field without changing their serialized form. Caller chain: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem` with `conversation` set → wire `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461) → `apply_work_item_rows` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38) → `reserve` (crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/conversation.rs:123) → `preceding_run` (conversation.rs:62) and `admission_matches` (conversation.rs:110). Shape: the head row id is `head_id` (conversation.rs:23) in the graph-incarnation-scoped NODES table; the request field `ConversationTurnAdmission` lives in crates/medulla-work-types/src/native_control.rs; generic writers are stopped by `row_guard.rs`. | Test: `crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/conversation/tests.rs::admission_reserves_one_ordered_turn_and_refusals_do_not_advance_head` (L2), `::reservation_survives_restart_without_reading_clock_order` (L2), `::generic_writes_cannot_forge_replace_or_delete_conversation_heads` (L2) and `::typed_admission_validates_context_without_changing_legacy_serialization` (L2). Replay, changed-body conflict and graph-incarnation isolation remain required in `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::conversation_turn_admission_replays_and_conflicts` (planned, L3). A successful WorkItem is not a completed-history receipt; the host must validate the exact completed checkpoint before execution. This child does not deliver transcript paging or served continuation. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.4` | **Repeated run admission replays the retained owner receipt.** Subsystem: work items. Caller: served `Method::SubmitWorkItem` → `dispatch_with_context` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158`) → `apply_native_work_item_family_operation` (`crates/medulla-store-apply/src/redb_store/store_native.rs:461`) → `apply_work_item_rows` (`crates/medulla-work-store/src/redb_store/work_item/dispatch.rs:38`) → the ledger's `begin_with_replay_identity` (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/commit.rs:134`). Rule: replay is the ledger's operation replay. No run-owner replay code exists, and none is added. `read_admission` (`run_owner.rs:70`) is the owner read of R028.4.1. Replay never calls it. A retry carries the same run, idempotency key and command, with a fresh request context and attempt nonce. The ledger resolves the stable operation identity to the recorded receipt. It returns `ReplayResolution::ReplayedResult`. A replay consumes only the fresh attempt nonce. It writes no batch, no outbox row, no node change and no version bump. A changed payload, method, authority scope, policy or principal under the same key refuses with `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. That code comes from `commit.rs:173`. A reused attempt nonce refuses with `REPLAY_NONCE_CONSUMED`. A key whose recorded batch is missing refuses with `CORRUPT_MUTATION_LEDGER`. A tampered operation row refuses with `typed replay receipt differs from durable row`. Already held, with their proof on main, so this row adds no test for them: fresh-nonce replay of the recorded result (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/replay.rs::a_fresh_nonce_over_the_same_stable_identity_replays_the_recorded_result`); conflict on a changed payload, method, scope or policy (`::a_changed_operation_identity_conflicts`); nonce reuse (`::the_same_nonce_is_rejected`); tampered operation and outbox rows (`::finalize_replay_receipt_rejects_persisted_operation_row_tampering`, `::replay_evidence_rejects_a_persisted_outbox_key_mismatch`); replay after reopen (`crates/medulla-transaction/src/tests/fault_restart.rs::a_committed_typed_receipt_reopens_and_replays_by_fresh_nonce`, `::a_typed_replay_row_without_a_durable_result_refuses_reopen`). Served, `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_binds_verified_owner_and_replays_without_row_changes` already holds these: `replayed` is true with the same `work_item_id`; the WorkItem node bytes are unchanged; a foreign owner gets `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; five changed requests refuse with the node unchanged; and replay works after a reopen. This row requires the three missing assertions. First, the replayed `SubmitWorkItemResult` equals the original in every field except `replayed`. Second, a changed `kind` under the same key refuses with exactly `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. Both hold before and after a restart. Third, an admission written by the previous patch build replays after this build migrates its store at open. The older-build store is written at test time by the binary that `MEDULLA_PREVIOUS_PATCH_BINARY` names, the R070.2 harness. No store file is committed. A 3.x retry of an admission recorded before 3.0 refuses `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`: the stable operation identity carries the protocol id, and no legacy replay exists (hard cutover). Budget: a replay costs one ledger point read, O(log N) time, and O(R) memory for an R-byte receipt. Benchmark: group `run_admission_replay` in `benches/work_item_claim.rs`, nightly, p99 under 5 ms over 10 000 WorkItems. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.1, MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R070.2. | Test: `tests/adopt_workitem_authenticated_replay.rs::run_admission_replay_returns_the_original_result_and_conflicts_on_change` (L3). It asserts full result equality except `replayed`, and the exact `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` for a changed `kind`, before and after reopen. Test: `tests/test_store_auto_migrate.py::test_previous_patch_run_admission_replays_after_upgrade` (L4). The previous patch engine admits a run. This build opens the directory and replays the admission. It gets `replayed` with the original `work_item_id`, `outbox_id` and `command_digest`. The leg runs when `MEDULLA_PREVIOUS_PATCH_BINARY` names that engine, the R070.2 rule. Bench: `benches/work_item_claim.rs::run_admission_replay` (nightly). |
Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R028.4.1

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R035.7.2` | **A replication connection creates a slot and streams the change feed.** Subsystem: wire listeners. Build row in `crates/medulla-wire` and `crates/medulla-mutation-types`. `message_loop::serve_connection` hands a connection whose startup parameter is `replication=database` to new `crates/medulla-wire/src/pgwire/replication.rs::serve_walsender` after login; a login without scope `replication:read` refuses with SQLSTATE `42501`; the connection's scopes are its wire login record's (`kg:read` and `kg:write` unless the grant names others, MEDULLA-ENGINE-PARITY-R007.4), so only an administrator-granted login holds it. Streaming pages `Method::ReadGraphChanges` (MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043.100) through `state.seams.dispatch` from the slot's `confirmed_cursor`, maps each change to `pgoutput` `Begin`/`Relation`/`Insert`/`Update`/`Delete`/`Commit` messages, and advances `confirmed_cursor` with `Method::KvCas` on each standby status update. An expired cursor ends the stream with `CURSOR_EXPIRED` (already registered in `crates/medulla-capabilities/src/error_routing.rs`). The outbound buffer is bounded at 64 MiB. `PgOperationalSurface::Replication` joins `SERVED_OPERATIONAL_SURFACES`. | `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R042.100.1` | **Measurement type and arithmetic.** Subsystem: benchmarks. Caller: `bench-claim admit` → `BenchmarkMeasurement::validate`. The measurement type carries no version suffix and has a `schema_version` field. Identity fields: benchmark case, configuration digest, dataset and query digests, release commit and hardware digest. Revision fields: model, provider, tokenizer and price-table digest. The price table is a versioned declarative table `{resource_kind -> unit, unit_price}` over `cpu_ms`, `bytes_read`, `bytes_written`, `network_bytes`, `model_tokens` and `storage_byte_hours`. It loads from configuration with a SHA-256 content digest. Cost is the sum over resource kinds of usage times `unit_price`. The digest is recorded on every receipt. Timing fields: request start, request end, model-ready time and inference elapsed ms. Count field: full input tokens. Throughput is `full_input_tokens * 1000 / inference_elapsed_ms`. The total uses the price-table currency and minor-unit exponent with round-half-even. Digests are SHA-256 as 64 lowercase hex characters. Caller chain: `medulla-server bench-claim admit` (an offline-tool subcommand, src/offline_tools/bench_claim.rs; usage `medulla-server bench-claim admit <measurement.json> --price-table <table.json> [--against <other.json>]`) → `BenchmarkMeasurement::validate`. If a member answers that replay with anything other than its stored terminal outcome, the fix is in that member's admit function named in the caller chain and is part of this row. Written-nothing is proven by an unchanged member read before and after the replay: `GetNode`, `KvGet`, `SELECT count(*)` and a series read. Budget: the replay answers under 5 ms p99 and allocates under 1 MiB. The L3 test measures it. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043.102. | Planned: `tests/mutation_batch_sole_envelope.rs::every_store_member_replays_its_terminal_outcome_across_restart` (L3): for each of `ApplyMutation`, `KvPut`, `Method::Sql` `INSERT` and `TsAppend`, the fresh-envelope replay returns the first response's result, the member read is unchanged, and the same holds after `reopen_after_sole_reference`. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R871` | **Tight agent loops stay inside a round-trip budget.** Caller: served memory and claim reads and writes over the local socket, and the same calls through the embedded engine. Rule: a single read or write must answer within p99 2 ms over the local socket on the reference host. `Batch` is a new served method, `Method::Batch { operations: Vec<BatchOperation> }`, that runs one transaction of at most 64 mixed operations (reads, writes and claims of the served memory and claim methods) and commits them together or not at all. It is separate from `BatchUpdate`, which keeps its bulk-ingest limits unchanged. A `Batch` of up to 64 small operations must answer within p99 10 ms. The embedded engine serves the same methods in process for harnesses that run beside it. The nightly group `decide/agent_loop` (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R178`) measures both paths. A `Batch` over 64 operations refuses with `BATCH_TOO_LARGE`. Caller chain: local socket → `dispatch_with_context` (crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary.rs:158) → memory and claim handlers; embedded engine → src/embedded.rs. Shape: `Method::Batch { operations: Vec<BatchOperation> }` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_00.rs`, member of `MethodWriteFamily::GraphElement`, with its result `BatchResult` (one `BatchOpResult` per operation, in order), its capability line (`node:write`, audited, atomic), its gateway route and a client method `MedullaClient.batch` (the recipe of `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R650.3`, including the exhaustive arm of `crates/medulla-server-api/src/batch_compile/canonical.rs`). `BatchOperation` is a closed set in `crates/medulla-types/src/wire_batch.rs`: `CreateNodeIfAbsent`, `CompareAndSetNodeFields`, `ClaimNext`, `Reinforce` and the one read `GetNodeProperties`, each carrying the fields of the served method of the same name. The handler validates the batch (`crates/medulla-core/src/batch_apply.rs::validate_batch`: size, then every blob) and the request boundary checks the scope of every inner operation (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/request_boundary/authorization.rs`, the refusal names the operation index); then ONE gateway commit runs `apply_batch_operations` in order, the same function durable replay and the embedded engine run. Created nodes carry the caller's owner id exactly as a top-level `CreateNodeIfAbsent` does; the operation cap is the registry knob `batch.max_operations` (default 64, range [1, 1024], operations; `crates/medulla-server-api/src/knob_config.rs`, `specs/public-engine-contract-and-release/contracts/knob-table.md`), read in the Batch gateway route (`crates/medulla-h-graph/src/graph_ops/gateway_graph_routes.rs`) and passed to `validate_batch`, so a batch over it returns the new `BATCH_TOO_LARGE` (added to `ServerErrorCode`) before any operation runs and writes nothing; a committed `Batch` whose replay faults (a read fault from `apply_batch_operations`) is a replay divergence: `durable_apply::apply` returns `ReplayDivergence` (registered code `REPLAY_DIVERGED`), and the Raft state machine, the embedded engine and the follower pull loop surface it, so a replaying store refuses to continue silently diverged; an empty `Batch` is refused `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `BatchUpdate` is not changed; the nightly group `decide/agent_loop` (a served read and write, p99 2 ms) and its sibling `decide/agent_loop_batch` (a served batch of 64, p99 10 ms) extend `benches/decide_budgets.rs`, budgeted in `benches/baselines/decide.json`; test file tests/test_agent_loop_batch_served.py. | Test: `tests/test_agent_loop_batch_served.py::test_batch_commits_once_and_refuses_oversize` (L4) and bench group `decide/agent_loop` (L6). |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.1` | **Model: the bundle manifest and parts.** Stage: model. Add `BundleManifest{bundle_version, graphs, parts, created_at, digest}` in new `crates/medulla-types/src/knowledge_bundle.rs`. `bundle_version` is the integer 1. `parts` lists, for each graph, `triples` (canonical N-Quads, as in MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R104.2), `embeddings` (vector id, model id, dimension, float32 values), `provenance` (the property-graph rows that carry no RDF identity, which the triples cannot hold: nodes whose id is not an RDF term and the edges that touch them, as JSON rows) and `documents` (SHA-256 digests with sizes, plus the `cas:sha256:` reference the rows hold). A document is every `cas:sha256:<64 hex>` reference in a visible node row that the caller can read; one it cannot read never enters the bundle. `digest` is SHA-256 over the canonical manifest bytes. Each part carries its own SHA-256. The archive is one tar stream, and the manifest is its first entry; the parts are `graphs/<index>/triples.nq`, `graphs/<index>/embeddings.json` and `graphs/<index>/provenance.json`, and each document is `documents/<hex>`. The embeddings part holds the graph's declared embedding space and its vectors. The manifest digest is `sha256:<hex>` of the manifest with an empty `digest`. Writes: none. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R043. Caller chain: `BundleExport` and `BundleImport` handlers (R877.2) construct and read `BundleManifest`. Shape: add `crates/medulla-types/src/knowledge_bundle.rs`. Acceptance: changing one byte of any part changes the manifest digest check result. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.2` | **Behavior: export reads one snapshot, import commits one batch.** Stage: admin. `BundleExport{graphs, include_embeddings, include_documents}` must read all parts from one snapshot of the caller's row-projected store, so a hidden node never enters a bundle. It must store the bundle as one blob through `BlobBegin`, `BlobChunkPut` and `BlobCommit` and return `{bundle_ref, manifest_digest, size_bytes}`. `BundleImport` answers one receipt, `BundleImportReceipt{graphs: [{graph, skipped}], triples, embeddings, provenance, documents}`: `graphs` lists each target graph in manifest order with `skipped` true for a graph left alone under `skip`; `triples`, `embeddings` and `provenance` count what the import wrote (a skipped graph counts none); `documents` counts the blobs stored. The arbora operation of MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.4 (`ARBORA-OPS-R923`) mirrors this receipt field for field. `BundleImport{bundle_ref, target_graph_prefix, on_conflict}` must read the blob and verify the manifest digest and every part digest before it writes. `on_conflict` is `refuse` or `skip`. The import must store documents by digest under the caller's blob scope and point every row at the stored reference (a stored blob's digest covers its owner), then create each target graph, commit its triples through the `AddTriples` gateway and its provenance rows and embeddings as one `BatchUpdate`, under the durable `importing` state of MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.3; a refused step deletes every graph the import created. A target graph that exists is refused with `refuse` and left alone with `skip`. It must re-run the admission checks of the target tenant's ontology. A bundle holds at most 5,000,000 triples and 4 GiB of documents. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.1. Shape: add `knowledge_bundle.rs` beside `namespace_migration.rs` in `crates/medulla-h-admin/src/`. Acceptance: a hidden row is in no part of the bundle, and a failed part leaves the target store unchanged. |
| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.3` | **Refusal: bundle codes.** Stage: admin. A digest mismatch refuses `BUNDLE_DIGEST_MISMATCH`. An unknown `bundle_version` refuses `BUNDLE_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED`. A size over the limits refuses `BUNDLE_TOO_LARGE`. A graph that exists with `on_conflict` `refuse` refuses `BUNDLE_GRAPH_EXISTS`. An embedding whose model id is not the id of the embedding space its graph declares (`undeclared` when it declares none) refuses `BUNDLE_EMBEDDING_MODEL_UNKNOWN`. Add the five codes to `ServerErrorCode`. Each refusal leaves the target unchanged. **Durable import state.** The import never relies on compensating deletes for its guarantee. Before it creates any graph it writes one `BundleImport` node (`bundle_import:<import_id>`, in the control graph `default_ingest_graph()`) holding `{import_id, tenant_id, graphs, state, started_ms, lease_until_ms}` with `state` `importing`; `import_id` is the SHA-256 of the tenant, the bundle reference, the prefix and the request idempotency key. The hiding is durable state of the graph itself, never a process-level set. `GraphMetaRecord` (`crates/medulla-store/src/redb_store/store_read.rs`) gains `importing: Option<String>`, the import id, absent once ready; `GraphEntry` and `CatalogRecord` (`crates/medulla-core/src/registry.rs`) carry it as `importing`. The graph's creation request names the import (`GraphLifecycleRequest::importing`), the marker is written to the registry row (`RedbBackend::set_graph_importing`) before the registry publishes the graph, and startup loads every marker with the catalog before a request is served. While the entry carries a marker, `check_graph_access` (`crates/medulla-server-auth/src/access.rs`), the one chokepoint every graph method and `ListGraphs` pass through, refuses it with the answer of any other denied graph; its `importing` argument is `entry.importing`. How the import's own writes pass: the check admits work running under the same import id as the marker (`access::importing::within_import(import_id, work)`, a task scope no request can set), never a caller identity, so there is still one comparison in one place and a second node or a restarted process hides the graph with no state of its own. A mutation context built from a gateway authorisation tuple carries `importing: None`: its request already passed the entry check at graph-operation entry. After the last step the import clears each graph's marker (`mark_graph_ready`: registry row first, then entry) and then one write of the node sets `state` `ready`; a crash between the two leaves `importing`, so the recovery pass deletes the whole import. A refused step deletes the graphs the import created and sets `aborted`. A crash leaves `importing` and the marked graphs hidden, with no recovery pass needed to hide them: the leader tick runs `medulla_h_jobs::bundle_import_recovery::recover_bundle_imports(state, now_ms)`, which for an `importing` record whose lease has lapsed deletes its graphs under the import id and sets `aborted`. Liveness is durable, never a process-level set: the record carries `lease_until_ms`, set to the start plus `IMPORT_GRACE_MS` (300,000 ms) when it is written, and the importer renews it every `IMPORT_RENEW_MS` (grace/3) through the engine clock, so another node's pass never deletes a live import and a crashed importer's lease lapses. The pass deletes only a lapsed lease, takes its clock as an input and is idempotent. Restart test: `tests/bundle_import_recovery_served.rs::a_restarted_process_hides_a_marked_graph_before_any_recovery_pass`. Crash test: `tests/bundle_import_recovery_served.rs::a_crashed_import_is_deleted_by_the_recovery_pass`. Depends: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R877.2. Caller chain: `BundleImport` handler (R877.2) → digest and version checks → refusal. Shape: edit `knowledge_bundle.rs` and `crates/medulla-types/src/contract/server_error_code.rs`. Reuse the refusal path of R877.2. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-admin-schema/src/knowledge_bundle.rs::tests::each_refusal_names_its_code_and_writes_nothing` (planned, L2); `tests/bundle_import_recovery_served.rs::a_crashed_import_is_deleted_by_the_recovery_pass` (planned, L3). Acceptance: five refusals return their codes, and a crashed import's graphs are hidden until its lease lapses and the recovery pass deletes them; a live lease is never deleted. |

| `MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R878` | **A served completion barrier replaces polling waits.** Subsystem: completion barrier. `Method::AwaitWatermark { resource, at_least, deadline_ms }` (scope `cdc:read`, a read) answers once the named asynchronous work has settled, so a caller waits on the engine and never sleeps and polls. `resource` is one of four names. `graph:<name>` is the committed-change watermark of a graph the caller may read (the head sequence of the graph's change feed). `catalog:<source>` is the catalog probe of a source the caller registered (0 until the probe has settled in `baselined`, `drifted` or `probe_failed`, then 1). `projection:<graph>` is the applied projection revision of a connector pack of the verified tenant that projects into `graph`: the pack head's `binding_revision` once the graph exposes that head's record, and one below it while the head is still projecting; a graph no pack of the tenant projects into refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. `projection:<consumer>@<graph>` is the graph version the named outbox consumer has acknowledged through on a graph the caller may read (0 before its first acknowledgement), so a caller that holds the previous value waits for the next acknowledged commit; the repository enrichment consumer is `repository-enrich-code-v1`. Pack projection completion and each enrichment acknowledgement wake the graph's waiters after the revision is readable. There is no resource that counts nodes by label, because such a count races with the commit that feeds it. The answer is `WatermarkAwaited { watermark, reached }`: the watermark held when the wait ended and whether it is at least `at_least`. `deadline_ms` is the longest wait, measured on the engine clock (`EngineClock`). The waiter is armed before each read, so a change that lands between the read and the wait still wakes it. A resource that names neither kind, or a source the caller did not register, refuses `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Under active row-level security a non-admin carrier is refused `ACCESS_DENIED`, because a watermark reveals the rate of filtered rows. Caller chain: the served request → `dispatch_streaming_methods_arm_0` (`crates/medulla-server-router/src/dispatch/router/data_plane.rs`) → `medulla_h_query::streaming::try_handle` → `await_watermark` (`crates/medulla-h-query/src/streaming.rs`). Shape: `Method::AwaitWatermark` in `crates/medulla-protocol-types/src/protocol/method/method_11.rs`; `WatermarkAwaited` and `WatermarkResource` in `crates/medulla-catalog-source-types/src/await_watermark.rs`; `AgentLibraryStore::connector_pack_projection_watermark` in `crates/medulla-persistence-records/src/connector_pack.rs`; the catalog probe signal `ForeignSourceCatalog::probe_ended` in `crates/medulla-server-ops/src/api/query_txn/foreign_catalog.rs`, woken when a probe job ends; the Python surface in `medulla/generated/` and `await_watermark` on both clients in `medulla/client.py`. Tests that wait on asynchronous projection, enrichment, import, restart or catalog-probe work call this method instead of looping on `asyncio.sleep` or `tokio::time::sleep`. | Test: `crates/medulla-h-query/src/streaming.rs::await_watermark_tests::the_barrier_answers_on_the_watermark_or_the_engine_clock_deadline` (L1). Test: `crates/medulla-catalog-source-types/src/await_watermark.rs::tests::a_resource_names_a_graph_or_a_source_and_nothing_else` (L1). Acceptance: a watermark at the target answers at once; a zero deadline answers the unmet watermark as not reached; a change landing during the wait wakes it. |
